#2241. Birmingham City set to welcome back James Beadle for Stoke City clash, but doubts remain over six-goal star

Birmingham City have gone into the November international break top of the Championship. BCFC have been really impressive to start the year, and have continued winning games, even with some injuries to key players.

Chris Davies’ side have gone the their last two games without goalkeeper James Beadle and top-scorer August Priske. However, as reported by Birmingham Live, Davies has provided an update on the pairs fitness going into the internationals.

“James (Beadle) definitely will be (back),” Davies confirmed but he couldn’t be so sure on Priske. “August (Priske) did everything to get ready but he couldn’t,” the Blues manager said.

“I presume he is going to make more strides in the next week or so. I’d hope he was back after the international break but I couldn’t be definite on that because it’s how it settles down.”

Davies also provided an update on Marc Leonard who is not part of the Birmingham City squad after being omitted at the start of the season following an Achilles injury. The BCFC boss said he is looking to be back in action after the New Year.

Both Beadle and Priske could return for Birmingham City’s trip to Stoke City on 21 November, but the striker’s availability would be the bigger boost.

Beadle is the established first-choice goalkeeper after missing the wins over Millwall and Swansea with a knock. Davies has confirmed he will be available for Stoke, although Ryan Allsop has impressed in his absence.

Priske’s return is less certain, but potentially more significant. The forward provides their main focal point in attack. Davies is hopeful the international break will give him enough time to recover, but has stopped short of guaranteeing his availability.

While BCFC have continued scoring without him, having their main goal threat back for such an important away fixture would be a considerable boost.

Priske has been a key player for Birmingham City this season and has really come into his own after a slow start last term. However, his injury has not slowed up the Blues as many might’ve thought.

Davies has adjusted his side well and in Priske’s absence, BCFC have still been an attacking threat. Max Bird has come into the side as part of the front four with great success.

Jay Stansfield has also found his scoring touch with three goals in his last two games. The Blues have adjusted very well to Priske’s absence, and while they were prefer to have their top-scorer in action, the club have shown they are still strong enough without him.

#2237. Taylor della Lazio non convocato dall’Olanda: Xavi spiega la scelta

Il selezionatore considera la sua esclusione la decisione più difficile e richiama la concorrenza a centrocampo.

Kenneth Taylor non è stato convocato dall’Olanda per i prossimi impegni internazionali. In conferenza stampa, il selezionatore Xavi ha definito la sua esclusione la decisione più difficile nella composizione della lista. La scelta arriva dopo le prime 11 presenze in Serie A del centrocampista con la Lazio, nelle quali ha segnato 2 gol e fornito 2 assist.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Nel suo primo scorcio di stagione in biancoceleste, l’ex Ajax ha contribuito con 2 reti e 2 assist nelle prime 11 uscite di campionato. Il suo rendimento è stato al centro delle domande rivolte a Xavi durante la presentazione dei prossimi appuntamenti dell’Olanda.

Il selezionatore ha riconosciuto le prestazioni del centrocampista in Italia, pur avendo scelto di non inserirlo nell’attuale lista dei convocati.

Xavi ha spiegato quanto sia stata complessa la decisione: «L’esclusione di Kenneth Taylor è stata in assoluto la decisione più difficile che ho dovuto prendere per questa lista di convocati». Nel reparto, ha aggiunto, l’Olanda dispone di molti centrocampisti di livello.

Secondo il selezionatore, quanto mostrato da Taylor avrebbe giustificato una convocazione. Il giocatore della Lazio resta comunque tra i profili seguiti in vista dei prossimi impegni della Nazionale olandese.

#2236. Cancellieri, meno spazio alla Lazio: Fiorentina attenta alla scadenza

Una cessione a gennaio consentirebbe ai biancocelesti di ricavare una somma dal cartellino dell'esterno.

Matteo Cancellieri potrebbe tornare nei piani della Fiorentina per il mercato invernale. Secondo quanto riportato da FiorentinaUno, L'esterno offensivo della Lazio, che nelle ultime settimane ha trovato poco spazio, sarebbe nuovamente seguito da Fabio Paratici. Un'eventuale operazione a gennaio resta un'ipotesi, legata anche alle scelte del club biancoceleste.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Il futuro a Roma del calciatore italiano, classe 2002, non appare scontato. Cancellieri non sembra più occupare una posizione centrale nelle scelte tecniche della Lazio e sta attraversando una fase complicata, nella quale giocare con continuità è diventato più difficile.

Alla situazione in campo si aggiunge quella contrattuale: l'accordo con i biancocelesti scade il 30 giugno 2027. Senza un rinnovo, la società dovrà decidere come affrontare gli ultimi mesi del rapporto. Anche il giocatore sarà chiamato a valutare quale soluzione possa offrirgli maggiore continuità.

Paratici conosce il profilo di Cancellieri e potrebbe scegliere tra due possibilità. La prima è provare ad anticipare l'operazione a gennaio, cercando un'intesa con la Lazio. L'altra è aspettare la fine della stagione per tentare di ingaggiare l'esterno a parametro zero.

Attendere consentirebbe alla Fiorentina di non sostenere il costo del cartellino, ma potrebbe esporla alla concorrenza di altre società. Non è la prima volta che il nome di Cancellieri viene associato ai viola: nelle precedenti finestre di mercato il club toscano aveva già mostrato interesse, considerandolo una possibile alternativa per il reparto offensivo.

Tra le caratteristiche prese in considerazione c'è la sua duttilità. Cancellieri può giocare soprattutto sulla corsia destra, ma anche in altre posizioni dell'attacco: una possibilità utile nella costruzione della rosa viola, che potrebbe cambiare volto.

#2234. Blackburn Rovers, Birmingham City track Chelsea starlet after Tony Mowbray snubbed summer loan deal

Blackburn Rovers and Birmingham City have both performed positively over the first quarter of the 2026/27 campaign, but they’ll have eyes on the January transfer window as an opportunity to strengthen the ranks further. Now, TEAMtalk has claimed one player of interest to both clubs is Kendry Paez.

The report states that Chelsea are actively looking for potential loan destinations for Ecuadorian prospect Paez. Rovers and the Blues are keen, but the 19-year-old also has admirers at Celtic and Trabzonspor, and he was eyed by Belgium’s KV Mechelen in the summer.

Paez was on the radar at Ewood Park in the last transfer window as was put to Tony Mowbray as a potential signing. However, the boss snubbed the deal, and the attacking midfielder has remained with Chelsea instead.

Paez was viewed as one of South America’s most exciting talents when he officially joined Chelsea in 2025. However, loans at RC Strasbourg and River Plate have not yielded the success hoped.

Blackburn Rovers were lined up as a potential destination in Paez’s bid to settle into English football, but after Mowbray opted against bringing him in, he has only played U21s football.

Paez has made six appearances across the Premier League 2 and EFL Trophy. He provided an assist in a 2-1 defeat to Wycombe Wanderers in September and played a little over an hour in a 2-0 triumph over Reading in the same cup competitions in October.

At 19, the Chelsea starlet is still young and has plenty of time to come good in England. But, Paez has struggled to impact games the way he did in his emergence in Ecuador with Independiente del Valle, so it remains to be seen if a half-season loan to the Championship can breathe life into his career again.

This applies to Birmingham City more given that they are the current Championship leaders, but clubs have to be careful with loan deals in the winter. Recruitment staff and managers alike need to be wary of how new signings will impact a thriving squad.

Loan players come in with a desire to play, but it can be tough to find opportunities in a thriving team. If that were to happen to Paez, it wouldn’t help his development and could bring players off the pitch too.

Blackburn Rovers aren’t in quite the same position as they’re 13th, but Mowbray has harnessed some strong squad spirit. The fact the boss has already snubbed the loan signing of Paez would raise questions over a winter deal for his services too.

Time will tell what comes of Paez’s winter transfer fate as Chelsea look for potential destinations.

#2233. Struggling £26m West Ham man eyed for escape transfer to South American giants

West Ham signed Taty Castellanos for a deal worth around £26m in January as they bid to salvage their Premier League status. WHUFC came up short despite their new striker netting six goals in 18 Premier League games, but his form has not continued in the Championship.

Now, a report from Argentine news outlet El Intransigente says Castellanos could find an escape route back to his home country. River Plate are keen on signing a new striker and the Hammers man sits atop their shortlist of targets.

Despite being from Argentina, Castellanos has never played senior football in his native. He has played in Chile, Uruguay, Spain and the United States, as well as in England with West Ham.

West Ham have not had an easy time in the Championship, dropping to 9th after Saturday’s loss to Blackburn Rovers. However, with 37 goals scored in 15 matches, they are still the league’s highest-scoring team by far.

Despite this, and his pedigree at a higher level, Castellanos has not found form in front of goal. He started the competitive campaign with a Carabao Cup brace against Portsmouth and scored in the same competition against Southampton in August too.

But, the 28-year-old has found the back of the net just once in the Championship, that being in the 6-0 home thumping of Wrexham. Castellanos has chipped in with a strong tally of five assists, but a total of 3.46 xG shows how he is underperforming in terms of goalscoring (FotMob).

It has seen him drop from the WHUFC starting lineup for four of the last six league games. Now, it brings questions over his future as River Plate lurk.

As highlighted, West Ham are scoring plenty of goals. However, they’re not coming from the strikers.

Of the natural no. 9’s on the books, Joel Piroe is the top scorer on four. Pablo earns praise for his physicality and work rate but two goals in 28 career games for WHUFC shows he leaves a lot to be desired in front of goal.

By selling Castellanos, the Hammers can raise funds and free up space in the squad for a new talisman to join. They can ease the burden on Piroe while Pablo can mainly be used in the impact substitute role he tends to be better in.

It could breathe new life into Scott Parker’s attack with clear threats out wide and in midfield like Jarrod Bowen, Edvin Austbo, Manor Solomon, Divine Mukasa and Mohamadou Kante.

#2230. Veltman’s West Ham Admission Highlights Parker’s First Big Problem

West Ham defender Joël Veltman has admitted that the Hammers’ habit of starting matches poorly must change, with Scott Parker facing an immediate challenge against Blackburn Rovers this afternoon.

Speaking in an interview with the club’s official website, Veltman acknowledged that Wednesday’s disastrous first half against Cardiff City was hardly an isolated incident. West Ham recovered from three goals down to draw 3-3, but the Dutchman knows they cannot keep relying on second-half comebacks.

“It wasn’t the first time we haven’t started so well,” admitted Veltman, who wants West Ham to begin against Blackburn with the same intensity they showed after the interval in Wales.

Personally, I think West Ham’s slow starts and early capitulations are definitely a mentality problem. Nothing demonstrated that more clearly than the transformation at half-time against Cardiff, when a team that had looked completely traumatised and shell-shocked suddenly emerged looking like an entirely different side.

Of course, some of that improvement was tactical, but I’m not entirely convinced how many tactics you can impart in 15 minutes. What Parker, Mark Noble and Jarrod Bowen clearly managed to do was give the players a lift, and the difference in their attitude was extraordinary.

I’ve thought from day one that this was probably the easiest improvement any new manager could make with the current group of players. If West Ham approach matches with a positive attitude and a genuine desire to win rather than playing fearfully, I suspect much of the problem could be addressed with a decent team talk.

That doesn’t mean tactics and team selection are irrelevant, but the players have already demonstrated they possess considerably more ability than their recent performances suggest. Parker’s first job is surely to convince them of that.

Veltman himself was absolutely brilliant during the second half against Cardiff, and I think he could become an important player under Parker. His composure and positioning at right-back could allow Jarrod Bowen the freedom to drift into central areas, where the West Ham captain caused absolute havoc on Wednesday evening.

Bowen was instrumental in the comeback, and having a dependable defender behind him could make that attacking freedom much easier to accommodate. It’s another reason why I’d like to see Veltman retain his place against Blackburn.

The defender certainly recognises the opportunity ahead, telling the club: “We want to go into another away game on Saturday and start where we finished.”

That really is the challenge for Parker. West Ham don’t need to discover some previously hidden ability between now and kick-off; they need to reproduce the confidence, aggression and positive mentality they finally showed during those remarkable 45 minutes against Cardiff.

Kris Gonzo (commonly known to the West Ham community as Gonzo) is the co-founder of Hammers Chat (established 2014) and the lead video content creator and columnist for Claret and Hugh.

With over a decade of professional sports media experience, he is a frequent West Ham United analyst and contributor for Sky Sports News, BBC Football Focus, and BBC Radio 5 Live. He previously served as an official video and content creator for West Ham United FC during the club's tenure at Upton Park and featured in the official documentary commemorating the historic final game at the Boleyn Ground.

A lifelong Hammer since the legendary 10-0 victory over Bury, when he isn't providing daily match analysis on the Hammers Chat YouTube Channel or broadcasting on X (@GONZObignose), he can be found walking his dog and restoring classic pinball machines.

#2228. Manchester United confirmed lineup: Amad & Yoro return to starting lineup

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United and Tottenham Hotspur return from the international break with plenty to prove when they meet at Old Trafford on Saturday.

Michael Carrick’s side have won just once in their last four domestic matches since beating Ipswich 5-2. A Carabao Cup defeat to Brighton was followed by a loss to 10-man Manchester City, before Manchester United drew with Everton and Fulham. Matheus Cunha’s late equaliser at Fulham rescued a point, but Manchester United still sit 12th.

Roberto De Zerbi’s side are bottom of the Premier League with only two points from their opening five games. Spurs have failed to score in four of those matches, while defeats against Aston Villa and Newcastle have added to the pressure.

Michael Carrick has named his starting eleven for the clash at Old Trafford, with Kobbie Mainoo and Youri Tielemans partnering in midfield as United look to get their season back on track.

Senne Lammens starts in goal, with Leny Yoro, Lisandro Martinez, Harry Maguire and Luke Shaw forming the back four. Carrick has opted for Mainoo and Tielemans in the middle, with Amad Diallo, Bruno Fernandes and Matheus Cunha tasked with supporting Bryan Mbeumo up front.

Leny Yoro had a brilliant international break where he made his debut for France. His performance on debut was enough for Zidane to praise the debutant.

Amad, after missing the start of the season with an injury, returns straightaway to the team.

Carlos Baleba starts on the bench and is expected to make his debut today. Marcus Rashford is also on the bench alongside Shea Lacey.

Lammens; Yoro, Martinez, Maguire, Shaw; Mainoo, Tielemans; Amad, Bruno Fernandes, Mbeumo; Cunha.

Mbeumo gets his place through the middle, while Amad and Cunha start on the wing after scoring the late equaliser against Fulham. Fernandes will be expected to start producing the numbers from last season after a lackluster start to the season, except against Ipswich, before the international break.

United need a response after a difficult run, but Tottenham arrive under pressure of their own. With both sides desperate for points, the starting lineups set the stage for an important afternoon at Old Trafford.

#2227. Manchester United player ratings: United failed to win despite advantage

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United and Tottenham Hotspur returned from the international break with plenty to prove after a disastrous start to the season.

United arrived with just one win, while Tottenham came to Old Trafford without a win.

Both teams had half-chances in the first half but little to show for them. Tottenham had the better chances with chances for Dominic Solanke, Connor Gallagher and Kudus, but none could take advantage of them.

Manchester United came close in the first half to score after Bryan Mbeumo raced towards the goal on a counter-attack to find Amad. He had a difficult shot, and eventually the shot was too weak to cause any damage. The first-half display was met by boos from the Old Trafford crowd at half-time and during the first half as well.

Solanke had another chance at the start of the second half but put it over the top, amid a wrestle with Lisandro Martinez in the six-yard box. It was United, however, who started the second half with promise.

Substitutions by Michael Carrick around the hour mark made the difference as United showed intent before Mbeumo got the goal off Cunha’s low cross. Luke Shaw was at the end of a scuffle with Bentancur, who was shown a red card for headbutting Shaw.

United failed to capitalize on their one-man advantage and seemed to sit on their 1-0 lead, but Solanke punished them with a header to level the game in injury time. The draw left United with just 6 points off their first six games. The result leaves United with their worst start in 40 years.

Lenne Sammens-6          Quiet afternoon for United no.1 till the goal, where he saw the ball go into the back of the net.

Leny Yoro-6       Defensively was fine but offered little upfront. Played out of position, and he showed good defensive awareness on the right back till his substitution.

Harry Maguire-5.5            Was decent in the first half but wasn’t much of an answer to Tottenham going forward in the second half

Lisandro Martinez-5      Took his eyes off the ball, resulting in conceding a late goal off Solanke.

Luke Shaw-6      Went up high on the pitch and remained a threat with his overlapping threat. Was involved in the scuffle that got Bentancur a red card for headbutting.

Kobbie Mainoo-6.5        Brilliant defensive tackle to deny Tottenham in the 68th minute. Remained composed in tight spaces against a physical Tottenham midfield.

Youri Tielemans-6          A decent performance and was solid throughout the minutes he was on the field

Bruno Fernandes-5        Another quiet and disappointing evening for the United captain after he failed to impress on a day he needed to get a result for United.

Amad-6              A positive return from injury, showed plenty of promise on the wing but failed to get past a resolute Tottenham side

Matheus Cunha-7           One of the liveliest options for United going forward. Provided an assist to Mbeumo to put United up front

Bryan Mbeumo-7.5        A brilliant finish off Matheus Cunha’s low ball to put United in front. Was dangerous going forward and denied a second on a counter by Kinsky off a brilliant long ball by Baleba.

Carlos Baleba (58’)-6.5  A decent debut overall for the Cameroon international. He put in a couple of dangerous balls up the field, including a clear ball to Mbeumo on a counter, who was denied by Kinsky.

Diogo Dalot (58’)-6      Provided a decent attacking threat when brought on around the hour mark.

Joshua Zirkzee (58’)-6   Showed plenty of promise and was a threat after he was brought in around the hour mark.

#2226. Michael Carrick reacts to Man Utd boos after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Michael Carrick admitted Manchester United’s 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur was a painful result, after his side failed to protect the lead against 10-man Tottenham at Old Trafford.

Manchester United looked set to claim three points when Bryan Mbeumo scored in the second half off Cunha’s low cross, but Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser in injury time left Carrick facing more questions on United’s performance and his future.

Speaking to BBC Match of the Day, the United manager felt his players improved after the break but failed to handle the situation when victory was within reach.

“It was a mixed game. We found it tough to start with. For a number of reasons, we were not quite there. You could see it was a challenging time,” Carrick said.

“But the lads grew into the game, in the second half I thought we were better in everything. To get the game as we wanted it, in such a good position – we should see it through. We have to manage those situations.”

United have now conceded two late goals that have cost them valuable points, something Carrick believes has made their position look worse than it might have otherwise.

Asked whether a lack of confidence had contributed to United’s slow start, he disagreed but admitted matches can quickly slip away from his side.

The boos at half-time and full-time also clearly affected him.

“It’s not nice, it hurts a lot because I care about this club. But I understand the disappointment, the reality is what it is, we need to get back to winning games.”

#2225. Bryan Mbeumo reacts to Man Utd draw with Tottenham

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Bryan Mbeumo admitted Manchester United have work to do after their frustrating 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, despite scoring a superb second-half goal at Old Trafford.

The Cameroon international put Manchester United ahead in the 73rd minute, finishing in the far corner after Matheus Cunha created the opportunity from a low cross. However, Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory, even though Tottenham had been reduced to 10 men following Rodrigo Bentancur’s red card.

Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2).

3 – Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2). Disadvantage? pic.twitter.com/DuSpvymZfu

Speaking to Sky Sports after the game, Mbeumo acknowledged that United had taken too long to find their rhythm.

“We took time to get into the game, but we played well in the second half. In the first half, that wasn’t us, we had to change. We tried to get at them,” he said.

Mbeumo recognised the threat Tottenham posed and admitted the result was disappointing, particularly given United’s position in the game before Solanke’s late goal.

“We know Spurs have good players who can be dangerous. It’s a bit disappointing, we need to work on things.”

United have struggled for consistency in the opening weeks of the season, and dropping points after taking the lead will do little to ease the pressure on Carrick. Mbeumo, however, insisted the team must look ahead rather than dwell on the setback.

“We need to keep going forward, as we have a lot of games.”

Michael Carrick was pleased with Mbeumo’s contribution, highlighting both the quality of his finish and the different roles he has played since joining United.

“Bryan’s done great. It was a fantastic finish again; he’s obviously played different roles for us but we know what he offers,” the United manager said.

“He’s got good quality and obviously the more goals he scores, the better. He’s doing really well for us.”

Mbeumo’s goal was one of the few bright moments in another difficult afternoon for United. Carrick’s team must now find a way to turn things around from here after just six points from six games.

#2224. Top 15 best away fans in the EFL ranked

When it comes to topics of discourse throughout the EFL sphere, few subjects promise to spark fiercer debate than arguments over which clubs have the best - and worst - away support.

Supporting your team at home is one thing, but committing to pocket-burning expenses and significant mileage travelling up and down the country in the name of fanfare takes on a new level of loyalty and is worn as a badge of honor by the clubs who make a particular impression in terraces on the road.

Factoring in a blend of noise and numbers while giving special attention to the teams that really clock up the miles, Football League World has decided to run the rule and rank the FIFTEEN best-supported clubs away from home in the EFL right now...

We're heading underway with Leicester City, who have always been historically well-followed despite never being a major-hitter in the realms of EFL fanfare.

Leicester's location within the East Midlands means few destinations are "too far", whereas relegation to League One for only the second time in the club's history makes for many interesting away days that, admittedly, supporters never thought they'd be embarking on.

Nonetheless, the Foxes often travel in numbers, are noisy when they're up for it and have already recorded two of the highest away attendances in the EFL this season after taking in excess of 4,000 fans to both Barnsley and MK Dons.

Southampton's fanbase, for all intents and purposes, is rather comparable to the contingent in support of Leicester, though it's the Hampshire outfit who just about gain an edge here.

The Saints' location doesn't always make away travel easy, yet that rarely stops them from bringing swathes of supporters on their trips with a decent atmosphere to boot.

Football League World has asked AI to rank the top 25 most demanding fanbases in British football right now

They posted the sixth-highest average away attendance in the Championship last season, and when those numbers are regularly accompanied by noise despite more arduous mileage than most, it's hard to resist giving Southampton their flowers.

West Bromwich Albion have always retained solid support both home and away amid plenty of local competition within the Black Country and wider West Midlands region, which is why it's only right to recognise their own standing on the road.

Once again, the majority of away trips fail to require lofty travel commitments and the club's West Midlands status makes it easy to reach most destinations by train.

That culminates in consistently respectable numbers with the capacity to make a serious racket when the good times are rolling or a local derby arrives, but whilst only six sides travelled better than the Baggies last term, the fact that multiple fellow Midlands clubs did prevents Albion from featuring higher.

Bolton Wanderers' support has been tested with rocky fortunes over the last decade, though with the tide having now truly turned following Steven Schumacher's appointment in January of last year, it has stood the test of time and we're now seeing a persistently-loyal fanbse rewarded for their efforts.

Only one club in League One recorded a higher average away attendance than Bolton, who averaged just under 2,000 supporters on their travels as Schumacher's side finally returned to the Championship, where their numbers and noise have both strengthened in tandem.

Bolton's home support can be outstanding, and their away standing is not too far behind either.

We're looking at both numbers and noise here, of course, and it's the latter criteria that truly does the lifting for Stockport County.

The Hatters have enjoyed a remarkable ascent up the footballing pyramid in recent seasons and that feel-good factor is palpable and reflected in the way that County fans support their side, proving to be something of a driving force towards aggressive unprecedented promotion pushes in League One.

At face value, the statistics would suggest that Stockport's away following doesn't necessarily pull up any trees, as five clubs recorded a superior average attendance to their 1,342 in 25/26.

However, the noise can be eclipsed by very few at its best, with Stockport fans rarely failing to create an almighty racket with the sort of volume, passion and intensity that speaks far louder than the numbers alone.

Another fanbase to have brought bodies and noise decibels aplenty to League One terraces last season was Cardiff City, who gained promotion at the very first time of asking with a fanbase that retained all of its volume and passion after returning to the third-tier for the first time in more than 20 years.

Cardiff's average away attendance of 1,716 was bettered by just two clubs, which is made all the impressive when you consider the fact that only a handful of sides throughout the EFL have to travel further than the Bluebirds.

Indeed, barring vociferous local derbies against Swansea and Bristol City, the Welsh outfit's closest Championship trip this season will be to Birmingham City - yet they regularly record stronger away averages than most while bringing even better noise and relish the 'us against them' mentality that comes with journeys across the border to England, which always carry an extra edge.

Derby County's home atmosphere is even better than what they conjure up on the road, though that's not to say that the Pride Park faithful fail to represent one of the EFL's stronger away fanbases.

In a season that saw John Eustace's side narrowly miss out on a play-off finish, the Rams' average away attendance comfortably exceeded the 2,000 figure and was among the best in the Championship.

Dismal form in the opening months of the current campaign has, quite naturally, reduced the volume of the Rams' ever-loyal travelling contingent, who are admittedly aided by a handy East Midlands location smack-bang in the centre of the country but have historically provided a level of away support bettered by few.

Bradford City's loud and proud fanbase has offered major fuel behind the club's rise from mid-table mediocrity in League Two to a now-consistent status as League One promotion contenders, having made the play-offs during their first season back at third-tier level last time out.

As the Bantams roared on to upset the odds and finish in fourth before losing to Bolton in the semi-finals, no team averaged a higher away attendance than City's 2,000+ figure.

West Yorkshire isn't exactly the easiest location to travel from for many fixtures down south either, even if Bradford were helped by a high volume of relatively-local northern encounters.

Either way, Bradford's home support gets immense credit and their fanbase, in terms of both noise and numbers, deserves just as much for what they get up to away from Valley Parade.

One of the strongest EFL fanbases full-stop comes in the form of Portsmouth, who, akin to many on this list, have stuck with their side through thick and thin before earning some relative rewards after persevering with no shortage of adversity.

John Mousinho's men are now establishing themselves as a second-tier staple once more, and even if Pompey aren't always making a splash on the pitch, you can be sure that they'll catch the eye - and the eardrums - away from it in the terraces.

Fratton Park is a well-documented cauldron of noise, but the same can be said for just about any away end that Portsmouth fans find themselves in.

Those same fans also have to contend with some of the most challenging travel requirements, with their closest trips after local foes Southampton arriving more than 80 miles away in London, yet that never prevents Pompey from creating serious noise on a consistent basis and travelling well after averaging more than 2,000 supporters on the road last time out.

The pride of travelling with both noise and body volume despite being so inconveniently located for just about any away fixture is a glory belonging to Plymouth Argyle, whose fanbase is one of the loudest, proudest and, in many ways, admirable, in all of the land.

It's remarkable that the Pilgrims feature so high when their closest away fixture in the 2026/27 term will be to Reading some 181 miles away following the relegation of rivals Exeter City to League Two, but the fact they still averaged away attendances comfortably in excess of the 1,000 benchmark while always managing to usurp their numbers with noise is testament to an especially-impressive Green Army contingent.

No team has to trek further on their travels, but Plymouth have always shown that it won't stop them.

#2223. Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp tear into Man Utd after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United have come under fresh criticism after their disappointing 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, with Sky Sports pundits Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp questioning the team’s performance and the challenges facing Michael Carrick.

Manchester United looked on course for a much-needed victory at Old Trafford after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half. However, despite Tottenham being reduced to 10 men, Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser denied Carrick’s side all three points.

Gary Neville felt Tottenham deserved the points, pointing to Solanke’s late goal and his impact as Tottenham captain.

“Tottenham deserved a point. That was a really important moment for Solanke. He’d missed some big chances and was captain for the first time,” Neville said on Sky Sports.

“They were the better team by far. If they’d left without a point, it would have been terrible for them.”

The former United defender also expressed concern about what the result means for Carrick, who faces increasing pressure to improve performances and results.

“Michael Carrick is left with a lot to think about. It was a worrying day in terms of the level of performance and a big week ahead.”

Manchester United spent massively in the summer to overhaul their midfield. However, Jamie Redknapp was critical of the performance he saw on the pitch.

“I do not see enough connections in that midfield, and they look devoid of any confidence, ideas, or patterns of play, playing out the back,” Redknapp said.

“Tottenham were just much better than them in every department, but that is a theme this season.”

The former Liverpool midfielder also questioned United’s energy and attacking organization, suggesting the squad is not strong enough to meet the demands placed upon it.

“Their energy levels are not good enough, and they look loose going forward; there was no real structure.”

Redknapp added that Carrick cannot fix every issue alone, insisting the manager needs backing to improve the squad.

“The squad is not good or strong enough, and Michael [Carrick] needs help in that respect.”

#2220. ‘Embarrassing themselves!’ Jamie Carragher hits back at Rio Ferdinand over Cristiano Ronaldo defence

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Jamie Carragher has taken aim at Rio Ferdinand for defending Cristiano Ronaldo following the Portuguese forward’s latest controversy with his national team.

Ronaldo has found himself at the centre of a growing dispute after abruptly leaving Portugal’s national team camp following a reported disagreement with head coach Jorge Jesus. The incident has prompted strong reactions from former teammates and pundits, with Ferdinand firmly siding with the Al-Nassr star.

Jamie Carragher, however, believes Ronaldo’s reputation should not shield him from criticism when his behaviour becomes difficult to defend.

Rio Ferdinand, who played alongside Cristiano Ronaldo at Manchester United between 2003 and 2009, criticised Jorge Jesus for the way he handled the situation.

Speaking on his Rio Presents podcast, the former United captain criticised Jesus for not showing respect to Cristiano Ronaldo.

“Cristiano Ronaldo was put in a very bad situation and was treated in an inappropriate way. He has an enormous history in football. Jorge Jesus did not deal with Ronaldo well and chose to treat him in that way, but I cannot find any explanation for it,” Ferdinand said.

Despite Jesus being Portugal’s manager, Ferdinand questioned his standing, comparing him with the likes of Sir Alex Ferguson and Pep Guardiola. To say the least, Ferdinand only embarrassed himself doing that.

Jamie Carragher saw the argument very differently and rightfully so.

Speaking on The Overlap Daily, the former Liverpool defender suggested that Ferdinand’s friendship with Ronaldo was influencing his reaction to the incident.

“People think it’s good to be close to people in the game, but this is where it becomes a problem,” Carragher explained.

He then called out both Ferdinand and broadcaster Piers Morgan, who has also been vocal in his support of Ronaldo.

“People like Piers Morgan and Rio Ferdinand, who are big people in the media whether you like them or not, are embarrassing themselves with the nonsense that they’re coming out with because they know Ronaldo. You can know someone and try to look after them, but you cannot defend the indefensible.”

Carragher believes Ronaldo’s achievements should not put the forward beyond criticism, particularly when his actions involve his national team and its management.

Gary Neville, who also played with Cristiano Ronaldo, has questioned Ronaldo’s handling of the situation, despite his longstanding affection for his former Manchester United teammate.

Neville argued that Ronaldo’s public response had made matters worse and the forward needed to accept criticism rather than expect others to defend him.

“With Cristiano, it’s difficult because I love him, but I can’t watch what happened at United in his second period and then see him make a statement like that without saying something. He has to be pulled up on that and told that it isn’t acceptable,” Neville said.

He also suggested Ronaldo’s extraordinary success, wealth and influence could have contributed to a sense that he was beyond challenge.

“When you have a billion pounds in the bank, the achievements he has had and the power he has, you feel you’re unbreakable, you feel you’re unstoppable,” Neville added.

Despite his criticism, Neville does not believe the episode will define Ronaldo’s legacy permanently. He expects the attention to fade once the forward retires, allowing his achievements on the pitch to take centre stage again.

#2219. What teams AI thinks will be in the EFL Championship in the 2050/51 season

It's always fun to predict how things will look in 20, 50 or even 100 years time, but how will the EFL tables look in 2050?

The Championship is ever-changing. The likes of Lincoln City and Wrexham have made their returns in recent years after decades away, while certain established Premier League clubs are always threatened with dropping into the second tier, too.

Predicting how things will look in the 2050/51 campaign, or in 24 seasons, is almost impossible. The second tier in the 2002/03 season saw Portsmouth win the title and be promoted alongside Leicester City and Wolves, but the likes of Gillingham, Walsall and Grimsby Town were all alongside them in the division.

So, which clubs does AI believe will be in the Championship come 2050? FLW has consulted ChatGPT to find out.

In an ideal world, Birmingham City would be in the Champions League, rather than the Championship, in the future, given the ambition of owner Tom Wagner and the amount of investment put into the Blues side in recent years.

That ownership and investment may not be there in 24 years, though, which could lead to this project crashing and burning.

By 2025, Birmingham will be more than settled in their new 62,000-capacity 'powerhouse stadium', but AI feels that it will be hosting second-tier football.

Blackburn Rovers are one of the longer-serving Championship clubs right now, with this their ninth successive campaign since they were promoted out of League One.

Recent years have seen them bounce between relegation battles and play-off pushes, and under Tony Mowbray, they look to be pretty steady. AI feels that they'll level out back in the second tier come 2050.

Bolton Wanderers' seven-year exile from the Championship came to an end this season, with their long-awaited promotion from League One serving as the final stage of their fightback from near liquidation back in 2019.

The passionate Trotters fanbase will be hoping that the coming years will see them become an established second-tier outfit with the prospect of making a return to the Premier League, and perhaps they will do that in the next couple of decades.

But by 2050/51, AI feels that Bolton will once again be a Championship side.

The first non-current Championship side in the list is Bradford City. The Bantams haven't been a second-tier side in over two decades, but AI feels that they will be in the next 24 years.

Many felt that the West Yorkshire outfit could have gotten into the Championship next season, and they still could, but the departure of Graham Alexander has definitely wounded them, and new boss Michael Duff still has a lot of work to do to get fans onside.

But this is a well-supported club on the rise, and even if it isn't this season, that promotion from League One doesn't seem too far away.

Bristol City being a Championship club at any point feels inevitable. This year marks 18 of the last 20 seasons spent at this level, so perhaps 24 years on from this term, they'll still be finishing 12th in the second tier.

The Robins are at the start of a project led by Michael Skubala, off the back of a summer with some decent investment in promising players across the field, most of whom have come from League One.

If all goes well, perhaps Bristol City could exceed this expectation and be a well-run Premier League side?

Cardiff City haven't historically been a side famed for sticking around in one division. Since the 1974/75 season, the longest stint they've had in one league was a ten-year one in the Championship before their promotion in 2012/13.

Their last second-tier run lasted just six years before relegation in 2024/25, and they answered that with promotion back at the first time of asking.

With Brian Barry-Murphy at the helm, the focus right now is on going back up again, but there'll be no doubt there will be a different direction and another man at the helm come 2050.

Bluebirds fans hope that they'll be able to take on what they hope is a Premier League job after BBM leaves, but AI sees them back in the second tier by 2050.

Charlton Athletic are in a bit of a sticky situation in the Championship right now, failing to win any of their last 11 games and losing four straight. Nathan Jones is praying that Burnley don't get any better anytime soon, else they risk falling into the bottom three.

24 seasons is a long time. Perhaps there's a drop back into League One on the horizon for the Addicks, but ChatGPT at least believes they'll get over that hump and get back into the second tier.

Derby County are another side in real peril at this moment in time. They're also a team that has become synonymous with the Championship over the past couple of decades.

Since being relegated from the Premier League in 2002, 24 years ago, they've spent one year back in the top flight, two in League One, and 21 in the second tier. Given the Rams have been a play-off side in quite a few of those seasons, too, it's quite an achievement for them not to have gone up more than once.

So, in the coming 24 years, it makes sense to put Derby in amongst this Championship lineup.

The second non-current Championship side in the 2050/51 lineup is a club many expect to be a second-tier outfit in 2027/28.

Huddersfield Town have enjoyed a brilliant start to life under Martin Drury, dominating both the attacking and defensive metrics. They have a duo in Joe Taylor and Derensili Sanches Fernandes who have 25 goal involvements between them, and they sit atop League One, looking less and less likely to relinquish that position anytime soon.

With ambitious owner Kevin Nagle overseeing things, he'll be hoping to be a Premier League regular by the time he's ready to relinquish control. By 2050, though, AI feels that the Terriers will be back in the second tier.

Both Bradford and Huddersfield will be joined by West Yorkshire rivals and the first current Premier League side on the list, Leeds United, which will make for some tasty matchups over the year for sure.

The Whites put in a brilliant showing in the second half of last season to retain their top-flight status, and after starting this year on the front foot, some Leeds fans are expressing some frustration at the number of draws their side are seemingly settling for.

Again, 24 years is a long time, so perhaps Daniel Farke can turn things around again and bring the Yorkshire giants back to being European contenders. But the German won't be in charge come 2050, and ChatGPT believes that they'll have slid back into the Championship by then.

#2218. Melvine Malard becomes Chelsea’s leading scorer as FOUR-goal start strengthens her claim to the No.9 role

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Melvine Malard’s Chelsea goals are beginning to provide a persuasive answer to the club’s centre-forward question.

Her opener in Sunday’s 3-0 victory over West Ham took the summer signing to four competitive goals, making her Chelsea’s leading scorer this season.

That total comprises two goals in Champions League qualifying and two in the Women’s Super League.

The circumstances make those contributions particularly useful. Across three scoring appearances, Malard has broken a deadlock, restored a lead and helped Chelsea put difficult matches beyond their opponents.

Three of those goals arrived while Chelsea were level or protecting a one-goal advantage.

That gives the early return significance beyond its size. Malard has repeatedly contributed while there was still a contest to resolve.

Sunday provided the latest example. Chelsea had plenty of possession but needed somebody to turn an opening into a lead.

Turn and finish 💥Melvine Malard finishes for @ChelseaFCW 🔵#BarclaysWSL pic.twitter.com/eicBNrYMYk

According to Chelsea’s official match report, the Blues reached the half-hour mark without a shot on target despite having 64 per cent possession.

Sjoeke Nüsken created the breakthrough by winning possession and finding Malard.

The striker received the ball with her back to goal, held her position, turned and finished into the bottom corner.

It was an important demonstration of what she can offer centrally. Chelsea could find her under pressure and still get a shot away, rather than needing to release her into open space.

Nüsken’s contribution deserves equal recognition. Her pressing created the opportunity that Malard converted.

The sequence offers Bompastor something useful to build on: winning possession higher up the pitch and finding a forward capable of finishing before the opposition recover.

Malard’s first three Chelsea goals came as a substitute.

Her two-goal competitive debut against Real Sociedad immediately demonstrated that capacity to change a match.

Nüsken released her for the goal that restored Chelsea’s lead at 3-2. Ellie Carpenter subsequently supplied her second, extending the advantage to three goals.

The official account of that European victory records two different routes to goal: a run beyond the defence and a finish from a delivery across the box.

Against Birmingham, her alertness produced another valuable contribution. Malard punished hesitation over a backpass to score Chelsea’s second, eight minutes after coming on, as detailed in the club’s match report.

Those appearances give Bompastor flexibility. Malard has already influenced matches when introduced against tiring opponents, while West Ham supplied a scoring return from the starting role.

Chelsea signed Malard from Manchester United on a four-year contract, bringing her back together with a coach who first identified her potential at 14.

Bompastor later coached her in Lyon’s senior side. Their relationship therefore predates this summer’s recruitment by years.

Speaking when Chelsea announced her signing, Malard said:

“My objective is to become better every single day and to win trophies.”

Her next task is sustaining this contribution across a longer run of matches.

Competition remains productive. Aggie Beever-Jones replaced her at West Ham and supplied Maika Hamano’s late goal, giving Bompastor another reason to value both forwards.

Malard’s strongest argument is the range of situations she has already influenced. She has attacked space, finished crosses, punished hesitation and scored with a defender close behind her.

Four goals offer an encouraging early return. The variety behind them gives Chelsea something substantial to develop.

#2217. Is Mainoo-Tielemans the right midfield option for Man Utd?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United drew 1-1 at home to Tottenham despite having an extra man advantage. The Reds saw Mbeumo put them in front, but Solanke scored a late leveller in injury time after Bentancur was shown a red card for headbutting Luke Shaw.

Manchester United’s worrisome season continues after accumulating just six points off the first six games, their first start in 40 years. But more than the results, what worries Manchester United is their midfield pairing.

United spent massively on their midfield rebuild and somehow can’t find the best combination in the midfield. Michael Carrick has gone with the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership, but they have failed to control the midfield. Individually, they both come with decent numbers, but collectively they fall short.

Mainoo and Tielemans struggled against Tottenham while closing the midfield and at times showed a lack of energy. On the day when nothing clicked for United, the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership particularly raised eyebrows.

Gary Neville has also raised serious concerns about Manchester United’s Mainoo-Tielemans partnership.

Neville was particularly unimpressed by the midfield balance, arguing that the problems went beyond one disappointing performance.

The former United captain stopped short of ruling out the combination altogether, but he suggested he has serious doubts about its suitability.

“Tielemans and Mainoo, I’m not calling time on it, that’s probably too drastic, but I’ve seen enough of it to think that’s not a midfield that’s dynamic enough to play at this football club in the Premier League.”

Former Spurs midfielder Redknapp felt United struggled to connect their midfield with the rest of the team during the opening stages.

He added: “I didn’t see enough connections in that midfield in the first hour. Baleba came on and did OK and showed a bit more.

“They look devoid of any confidence, any idea, any patterns of play, playing out from the back. Tottenham were much better than them in every single department.”

While Carlos Baleba was a particular highlight on the day, he showed great potential when brought on. He brought a new sense of energy in the midfield and sent a few balls over the top for a couple of quick counterattacks as well.

Redknapp also pointed to the physical demands of modern Premier League football, arguing that United cannot afford to be outworked by their opponents.

Tottenham ran a staggering 5 km more than Manchester United, something Redknapp felt was a pattern.

"Spurs ran 5km more than Man United… it's like that EVERY GAME!"Jamie Redknapp is less than impressed with the lack of ground covered this season by Manchester United 😬 pic.twitter.com/BxWkFuyGJ1

“There is a pattern that I don’t really like when I watch teams, and that’s when every time the stats don’t lie and the other team runs more than you.

“I feel in Premier League football right now there are a couple of non-negotiables, and one is you have to run more than the other team if you want to win or at least get close enough. Tonight Spurs ran 5km more than Manchester United.”

He believes Tottenham’s pressing exposed the lack of pace in United’s midfield, making it difficult for the hosts to move the ball safely through the centre.

“In terms of how they played today, I think they have an issue with Tielemans and Mainoo in midfield. I don’t see enough energy in there. Baleba will add a lot and will give them a tremendous amount of energy but right now, when things aren’t going well… and Tottenham knew it!

“Every time United played the ball out from the back they pounced. They pressed them because they knew United don’t have the pace and speed in midfield to get away.”

“In isolation, one of them is absolutely fine, the two of them together doesn’t work for me. They haven’t got the pace or the energy to get around the pitch for 90 minutes.”

#2216. Danny Drinkwater on Chelsea move he ‘could never turn down’

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Danny Drinkwater Chelsea story still carries a sting. Five years at Stamford Bridge gave him plenty of time to think about how it all turned out, and his own verdict is not a happy one.

Drinkwater opened up in a Truth or Padel appearance alongside David Ornstein. He said the move was one he could never have turned down, even with the benefit of hindsight. “No, I don’t regret moving. I regret, in hindsight, how it’s turned out. I think I could never turn it down,” he said.

Strip away the headlines and the numbers alone tell an uncomfortable story. Drinkwater spent five years on Chelsea’s books. In that time he made just 23 appearances, according to the original interview report.

Do the maths and it barely comes to five games a year. That is a thin return for a central midfielder signed to compete for a first-team place. It is also the kind of record that rarely sits well at a club of Chelsea’s size and ambition.

Chelsea’s best attacking spells over a similar stretch of seasons look far more productive, as our striker rankings show. Set next to those numbers, Drinkwater’s five quiet years stand out even more starkly.

Five years. Twenty three games. These numbers alone explains why this transfer still gets talked about by Drinkwater, long after the headlines faded.

The numbers only tell part of the story. A string of incidents turned a quiet, underwhelming spell into something far more pointed.

Drinkwater had a training-ground bust-up while out on loan. He was also involved in an on-pitch brawl with Chelsea’s own under-23s side. Flashpoints between a first-team player and a development squad are rare at any club, let alone one with Chelsea’s standards.

Taken alone, either incident might have passed as a bad day. Taken together, it became harder to see Drinkwater simply as an unlucky squad player who could not catch a break.

The most serious moment came during his time at Aston Villa. Drinkwater headbutted team-mate Jota and was fined two weeks’ wages for it. He says the consequences at Chelsea did not stop there.

“They didn’t formally ban me, but I got removed from the first team building after the incident at Villa,” Drinkwater said.

One off-field incident, at another club, pushed him out of the manager’s day-to-day plans back home. No formal punishment at Chelsea followed. The effect was much the same.

Chelsea fans saw a similar breakdown of trust play out when Graham Potter struggled to win over the dressing room during another difficult Chelsea transition. Trust can break down fast once results or behaviour turn against the person at the centre of it.

Drinkwater’s comments matter beyond his own career. They are a reminder that saying yes to a big move does not guarantee it will work once a player walks through the door.

That tension has not gone away. Chelsea’s current transfer business, covered in our transfer watch, shows the club still weighing big calls on players whose futures depend on more than form alone.

Squad management under Xabi Alonso, assessed in our bomb squad verdict, raises a familiar issue. Players who fall outside a manager’s plans rarely find a quick way back in, whether the exile is formal or not.

Drinkwater never found his way back into Chelsea’s plans after the Villa incident. His account of the Danny Drinkwater Chelsea years stands as a blunt warning. A move that looks impossible to refuse can still become one of the hardest spells of a career.

#2215. One lesson Senne Lammens learned after Tottenham’s late equaliser

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Senne Lammens believes Manchester United must learn to finish games off after their late collapse against Tottenham Hotspur.

The result left the goalkeeper frustrated at Old Trafford despite leading the game and with a player more on the field.

Manchester United looked set for three points after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half, but Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory.

Speaking to MUTV, Senne Lammens admitted the team had struggled early on but had done enough after the break to put themselves in a winning position.

“Football can be a bit cruel at times,” the Belgian goalkeeper said. “There are a lot of ups and downs, especially in a game like that.”

Senne Lammens felt United failed to take advantage of Tottenham’s red card, allowing the visitors too much space to find a late equaliser.

“We’ve won a lot of games against 10 men [in the past], but you also know there can be a tricky situation when you’re playing against 10 men, because they have nothing to lose and they know that.”

He added that United should have scored again to put the result beyond doubt or, at the very least, prevented Tottenham from creating further chances.

“I think that’s what we did wrong. We gave them a little bit too much breathing room to create those chances, and then one ball has to go right for them, one bounce has to go their way, and they can score like that.”

The goalkeeper also pointed to similarities with United’s earlier disappointment against Everton, where points slipped away late in the match.

“You feel like those games just slip away from you, and there are one or two situations where you don’t really have control,” he said.

“I think there’s a lot to learn from today. It wasn’t all negative, not at all, but especially the way it ended left a very bad feeling.”

“I think the easy answer is that we have to be able to kill the game faster and not put ourselves in that position to give them a chance.”

Despite the frustration, Lammens said there were positives to take from the performance. However, the late goal had left a bitter feeling, and United now need to turn those lessons into better results.

#2214. How Dave Challinor has reacted to shock Sheffield Wednesday loss to Oldham

Dave Challinor’s reaction to Sheffield Wednesday’s FA Cup defeat to Oldham Athletic has received a big reaction from supporters.

The new Owls boss oversaw his first game in charge on Sunday, with the team suffering defeat away to the League Two side.

Calum Kavanagh put the Latics ahead in the 66th minute, but Davis Keillor-Dunn responded to bring the game to extra-time.

But Will Sutton’s strike in the second half of the extended period gave the hosts a 2-1 advantage that Challinor’s side were unable to overcome.

The Yorkshire outfit have been eliminated from the FA Cup in the first round, highlighting the size of the task at hand for the new head coach.

The League One side will be aiming to compete for promotion back to the Championship this season, having brought in the former Stockport County boss in place of Henrik Pedersen.

Challinor has expressed his disappointment at the performance from his Sheffield Wednesday side, even though he knew that there was never going to be immediate progress after just a few days in charge.

He criticised the first half performance for being too passive, before praising the improvement in the second half, despite ultimately conceding two goals to suffer defeat.

“Really disappointed,” said Challinor, via the club’s official X account.

Could Josh Windass return to Sheffield Wednesday when his Wrexham contract expires in 2028?

“It was always going to be the case, it was never going to be linear in terms of what progress looks like.

“Probably knew things coming into the game, and coming into the club, and that’s reinforced those things, and the things that need to change.

“We’re in a position, we’re a little bit hamstrung in terms of the team we can pick, and who can play certain minutes, and all those types of things, which is almost like pre-season, not middle of November in the middle of a League One season.

“First half, miles too passive, like a testimonial.

Is Dave Challinor the man to bring Sheffield Wednesday forward? Comment your opinion below

“And players maybe being a little bit protective because of what the back end of the game looks like.

“Second half, much, much better, having our best spell and obviously concede two really poor goals, but that’s nothing to take away from Oldham, who deservedly go through.”

Challinor highlighted a particular issue with fitness within the squad, suggesting that standards have to improve if players are going to reach the goals they’ve set out for their careers.

“Great reaction to that, and we’re on the front foot, and like I say there will be pleasing stuff from the second half,” he continued.

“The extra time period, you know what it’s like, it can go either way.

“Physically, we’ve got to be miles better.

“You look at, I’ll use Barry [Bannan] as an example, but he’s 36-years-old and he’s running around all over the place, and we’ve got players that will be aspirational in terms of where they want to go and play that are getting cramps left, right and centre.

“Dish has probably got a good excuse for that, he’s not played, but the rest, if we want to play like what we all agree a Sheffield Wednesday team should look like, and be front-foot, we need to have those behaviours every single day.

“In order when we come out here and play, the supporters see and we represent them in a better form than what we did tonight.”

Sheffield Wednesday are now winless in their last five games in all competitions, with their last victory coming against AFC Wimbledon on 17 October.

Sheffield Wednesday fans have backed Challinor’s assessment of the game and the squad’s fitness issues.

Supporters are behind the new manager, suggesting that they think he’s surprised at the fitness levels of the players.

Fans are optimistic that the 51-year-old is going to raise standards, believing that the players have had it easy for far too long.

The supporters are also pleased to see that the new manager is giving an honest assessment of the game, instead of offering empty platitudes.

Many are hopeful that Challinor will now work on improving the overall fitness level of the squad, with some wondering if the issue has caused the injury problems of the recent past.

Fans are willing to give the former Stockport boss time to turn things around, claiming that it will be a slow process to fix the problems with the team.

Supporters believe that Challinor knows what he’s talking about, and are encouraged by his angry reaction to the loss.

Next up for the Owls in the league is a home clash against Plymouth Argyle on 14 November in a 3pm kick-off.

The defeat to Oldham has revealed to Challinor the size of the task at hand, and he knows it won’t be a quick process in turning things around.

Sheffield Wednesday reached a significant low point last season, and it was always naive to think that it could all be undone in just one year.

But Challinor is an exciting appointment because he knows what it takes to compete to a high standard at this level, and he can reintroduce that to the club after a challenging year in which the Owls came out of administration.

Despite the loss, fans seem optimistic about the future under the new manager, and these comments have backed that belief.

#2213. Blackburn Rovers 0 – 0 West Ham United | Half time report

A cold crisp afternoon, perfect for football saw West Ham seemingly doing their best to get into their opponent’s half after the last three weeks’ slow, ponderous starts. The Hammers spent the early moments camped in Blackburn’s half, accompanied by the noise of 7000 visiting fans making Ewood Park sound more like London Stadium on a good day.

Most of West Ham’s early movement was through Mayers and Austbø down the left, the two combining well with the full back pushed right forward in an adventurous set up by Scott Parker.

However it was Blackburn who almost struck first thanks to an overlap down their right and a shot that looped agonisingly over Hermansens’ goal. Mayers responded by fizzing one cross just out of reach of Piroe -nil nil at 20 minutes.

Engels, Austbo and Mayers combined well as the Hammers attacked repeatedly down Blackburn’s left, Austbø impressing in place of Solomon. Half an hour gone and few scares for West Ham:  A more competitive, aggressive start than in recent weeks. Piroe had one ball played through but his lack of pace allowed defenders to crowd him out.

The half continued in the same vein, West Ham looking well capable of scoring with most of the attacking coming down the flanks, Mayers on the overlap giving Scott Parker a new dimension to his Hammers offensive work.

Worryingly, Engels went over on his ankle just on the stroke of half time –  the teams went in at 0 – 0, a far more measured, controlled half than West Ham fans have been treated to in recent weeks.

Glad Piroe playing, even though he is not ripping up any trees. Like others have said, good to have a no. 9 have a run of games and players around learn how he plays, where to find him.

Mayers under pressure and coping fine also with overlapping runs and creating some danger, great, but not many in the box each time. A bit of patience perhaps…

#2212. Manchester United defence: Why Carrick must trust Yoro and Heaven

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United endured another embarrassing draw against 10-man Tottenham after failing to register their second win despite bringing in a plethora of players.

Manchester United invested heavily last season in their attacking options, bringing in Bryan Mbeumo and Matheus Cunha.

This season, they overhauled their midfield. United spent massively on Carlos Baleba, Youri Tielemans and Andrey Santos. Despite spending massively, they have failed to produce the control they would have expected in the midfield. The lack of control was evident in the recent Tottenham game, where Mainoo-Tielemans partnership failed to control the game collectively.

The midfield question was covered in our recent report as well, highlighting a lack of control and carried comments from Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp.

The bigger question is when United will strengthen their defence. As per our previous report as well, Manchester United are planning to overhaul their defence in the upcoming transfer windows.

United were heavily linked with several left backs but eventually failed to bring any one. The situation left Luke Shaw as the only senior option in the position, while Patrick Dorgu is used upfront by Michael Carrick.

Harry Amass is another option for Michael Carrick, but he is only here because of a failed transfer move. He has been rarely used at left back as Dalot and Mazraoui get the nod in the position ahead of him.

The situation at right-back isn’t all good either. Diogo Dalot is the first option there, but he rarely provides the assurance that puts Michael Carrick at ease. Noussair Mazraoui is the second choice at right-back, but his attacking numbers aren’t great either.

Harry Maguire and Lisandro Martinez form up United’s centre-back pairing while Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven are used all over the defensive line in rotation when needed. Yoro was used at right-back against Tottenham while Heaven arrived as left-back towards the end of the game.

Manchester United’s season hasn’t been defensively strong or anywhere near being good. They have conceded goals in all Premier League games and only kept one clean sheet against Sabah in the Champions League.

Lisandro Martinez has come under scrutiny after errors on four occasions. Against Ipswich Town, Manchester United won 5-2, but his error resulted in Ipswich’s second goal.

His own goal against Fulham gave away two points at Craven Cottage in the 1-1 draw. Another error led to Solanke’s goal against Tottenham, when he took his eyes off the ball.

His injury concerns are another aspect, and maybe Michael Carrick needs to make a decision.

Manchester United’s success depended heavily on youth over the years. When pundits questioned them, Sir Alex Ferguson gave them the chance to prove them wrong. Alan Hansen made the famous quote “You can’t win anything with kids” on the BBC’s Match of the Day on August 19, 1995. United did exactly that, played youth and went on to win everything.

Michael Carrick needs to do exactly that. He needs to trust youth and allow them to make mistakes. Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven arrived as massive potentials, but they haven’t been given the license to express themselves, without fearing for their places. Both the youngsters have rarely started together or in a stretch of games. It is maybe time for them to take their chances.

Michael Carrick needs to keep Harry Maguire as a senior experienced figure around them. There is no doubt over Martinez’s ability, but he is not the answer to United’s long-term problem. United need to blend youth with experience as their identity moving forward. Lisandro Martinez perhaps isn’t the answer to United’s problem.

A defensive revival expected in the upcoming transfer windows needs to happen now. It must happen with chances to the Youth. Michael Carrick needs to be brave enough and throw the youngsters in the mix. What it will bring is a long-term solution. They need to experience the errors and pressures that will shape them in football.

#2211. Keira Walsh adds another Chelsea weapon as former Arsenal target scores THIRD goal of the season

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Keira Walsh is giving Chelsea opponents another decision to make: protect the penalty area or step out to stop her shooting.

Her goal in Sunday’s 3-0 victory at West Ham took her to three for the season across all competitions. Each has come from outside the box.

For a player whose passing helps organise Chelsea’s attacks, that threat adds another way to influence matches. Recent performances show her contributing both to the construction of chances and their finish.

Against Sociedad, Walsh struck from distance after a corner, helping Chelsea complete their passage into the Champions League’s league phase.

The club’s account of that qualifying victory describes a tight encounter in which clear opportunities were limited.

At Manchester United, Lucy Bronze found her near the edge of the box and Walsh drove her finish into the bottom corner.

She also supplied Lauren James’ goal that afternoon, as Chelsea’s match report records. It was a useful illustration of the choices she offers: attempt the finish herself or release somebody closer to goal.

West Ham provided another example of that balance.

Walsh had already struck the post before Alyssa Thompson carried the ball in from the left and found Wieke Kaptein. Kaptein moved it on to Walsh, whose finish gave Chelsea breathing space.

The official report also records an earlier Walsh run down the channel and delivery towards Sjoeke Nüsken.

Her involvement therefore extended beyond waiting for opportunities to shoot. She helped Chelsea approach the penalty area from different positions.

There was a similar reward against Arsenal a week earlier. Walsh’s saved effort fell to Thompson for the only goal, as covered in our report of Chelsea’s derby victory.

That sequence illustrates why a dangerous shot can matter even when the goalkeeper stops it. Chelsea had a runner ready to attack the rebound.

Our assessment is that Walsh’s shooting gives defenders an additional problem. Closing her down can open a passing route; allowing her time leaves the goalkeeper exposed to a player already finding her range.

Walsh has also explained why she feels comfortable alongside Potter.

Speaking after the Arsenal victory in an interview published by Chelsea, she said:

“Lexi is really intelligent. She makes me feel really comfortable when I’m playing next to her.”

Her wider assessment emphasised collective responsibility, with team-mates covering for each other and working to make Chelsea harder to beat.

That matters when assessing midfield influence. Walsh’s freedom to help an attack depends partly on the positioning and decisions of those around her.

Potter subsequently started alongside her in Chelsea’s confirmed team against Lyon, giving their partnership another demanding European assignment.

The encouraging feature is the trust Walsh describes. Bompastor has an experienced organiser working with a younger midfielder whose reading of the game she clearly values.

BBC Sport reported that Barcelona rejected Arsenal’s bid for Walsh in September 2024.

Chelsea completed her signing in January 2025 on a four-and-a-half-year contract. Her official club profile outlines the technical quality and ability to dictate tempo that underpinned that recruitment.

Those qualities remain central to her value. Three early goals strengthen the attacking contribution around them, without establishing that she has changed position or should abandon her deeper responsibilities.

For Bompastor, the opportunity is to preserve that balance: Walsh helping Chelsea progress through midfield, supporting those around her and recognising when the space opens for a finish.

Manchester United are reportedly looking at options beyond Michael Carrick after the club believes he is not the right option for United.

Former Germany manager Julian Nagelsmann is among the names linked with a possible move to Old Trafford if United

Indykaila, a famous insider account on X, reported that Manchester United have started the process of finding their next head coach. Nagelsmann’s representatives are said to be willing to hear what the club has planned before deciding whether the project would suit the German manager.

United have not announced any change in their managerial plans, so the report remains unconfirmed. Carrick is still in charge, but his results at the start of the new season have given the club plenty to think about.

Carrick’s achievements last season bought him the permanent role, but the opening weeks of this campaign have left little room for further slip-ups.
