#2208. Blackburn Rovers 2 – 1 West Ham United | Final whistle, first impressions as new manager bounce falls flat

West Ham managed to overturn their recent sequence of dreadful, lethargic starts with bright first half performance against Blackburn Rovers. Both sides had their chances but West Ham’s attack dominated the first half, largely through Austbø and Ezra Mayers down the left hand side.

The Hammers set up with Engels the deepest midfielder sitting in front of the back four and Kanté plus Mukasa the more forward of the trio. It seemed to protect the back line a little better as, apart from one looped shot over Hermansen’s goal, Blackburn’s threat was contained during the first 45 minutes.

Three or four times in the first ’45 Mayers overlapped after combining with Austbø – looking far more potent a threat than Solomon who he’d replaced – and got excellent crosses in from the left, an attacking option that had been lacking with Morato as the full back.

Piroe headed against the post early in the second half after Austbo – again – twisted and turned on the left, and Engles crashed the rebound just wide. Waves of pressure developed on the Blackburn goal as the Hammers responded to their half time talk but against the run of play from a break down the other end, Blackburn took the lead through Gudjohnson who headed through Hermansen’s legs. West Ham behind again.

Parker responded with Solomon for Austbø who’d run his socks off for an hour and Taty appearing in place of Kanté.

Controversy as Rovers scored a second with Kilman prostrate after a head collision – Dapo Afolayan the ex West Ham boy fired one into the corner leaving West Ham with a mountain to climb. It must be said, naive defending from West Ham.

Joel Piroe grabbed one back after a lucky bounce sat nicely for him in the box: 2 – 1 with eight minutes to go and the Ewood Park atmosphere changed, the home fans nervy and the Hammers fans in full voice. The minutes ticked away and West Ham struggled to get the ball towards Blackburn’s box: Orford, a late substitute, very fortunate to avoid conceding a penalty in the closing minutes – once again the lack of VAR coming to West Ham’s rescue.

Five minutes of added time, West Ham looking as if they had neither the urgency or the ability to surge forward again, fiddling about on the edge of their own box with Hermansen being booed every time he touched the ball. One last chance as Soucek headed straight at the ‘keeper in the last few seconds and that was that. Poor. Very poor. So much possession and so little to show for it.

#2207. Sheffield United rival Portsmouth in race for star who can produce 'absolutely crazy numbers'

Sheffield United are already looking ahead to the January transfer window, as Chris Wilder prepares to strengthen areas of weakness throughout the Blades squad, though they may well face some strong competition from division rivals Portsmouth.

Sheffield United are looking forward to the January transfer window and have set their sights on Belgian youth international Renzo Tytens to reinforce their defensive options.

However, Championship rivals Portsmouth are also heavy admirers of the current Lyngby left-back, as this saga is set to heat up as the winter window draws ever closer.

Football League World has asked AI to rank the top nine toughest fanbases in the EFL Championship right now from least to most

According to Rudy Galetti's Substack, the Belgian journalist has reported that Portsmouth are the current frontrunners for the signature of Tytens, though they face a threat from Sheffield United and French side Toulouse.

Tytens, 21, came through the youth ranks of Dutch outfit PSV Eindhoven before spending a brief period with Club Brugge's youth team between the summer of 2024 and January 2026, when he moved to Denmark with Lyngby.

Since joining Lyngby, he has helped his side achieve promotion back to the top flight and locked down the left-back spot as his own, with his performances in the Superligaen attracting interest across Europe.

Speaking to Danish publication Tipsbladet, former Brentford defender and current Lyngby coach, Andreas Bjelland, was full of praise for Tytens, as he said: "Our scouting department and sports director have done a fantastic job finding Renzo, who we think is a good fit. He's not Gustav Mortensen, but he's a bit like that.

"He can produce some absolutely crazy numbers and has a good foot and has proven to be really, really important for us already."

Sheffield United are also reportedly considering Motherwell's Emmanuel Longelo, though no formal discussions have taken place at this stage for either player.

Sheffield United's interest in Tytens and Longelo will surely come as a surprise to many, with each of Harrison Burrows, Sam McCallum, and Rhys Norrington-Davies already battling for a starting berth at left-back at Bramall Lane.

However, Wilder may well be looking to the future, and with Norrington-Davies out of contract next summer, a gap in the Blades' squad may open up for Tytens, though surely one of Burrows or McCallum must leave also.

Portsmouth, meanwhile, have Jayden Meghoma and Connor Ogilvie battling for a place in the Pompey side under John Mousinho, though with Meghoma only on loan from Brentford and Ogilvie now 30, it's hardly a shock the South Coast outfit may be looking to the future with Tytens.

Portsmouth are the favourites for his signature as things stand, and having spent time in two prestigious academies and gaining experience in a top-flight European league in three different countries already, Tytens could well be making England his next destination sooner rather than later.

What do you make of this potential addition, Portsmouth and Sheffield United fans? Would you like to see Tytens head to your club in January, or should you be looking elsewhere? Share your thoughts below!

Now into the midst of a two-week international break, clubs across the Championship will take a step back to assess their current squads and begin making preparations for the January window, as Sheffield United and Portsmouth will be no different.

Both sit just outside of the play-off positions as things stand, and both Wilder and Mousinho will feel as though there is no reason their sides can't go on and claim one of those top eight spots.

The winter window will provide a chance for both to strengthen areas of weakness, as a move for Tytens appears to be on the cards for both.

#2206. Man Utd told £50m offer could tempt Fulham to sell highly rated teenager

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United could face an expensive battle to sign Fulham youngster Josh King, with offers exceeding £50million potentially enough to persuade the London club to consider a sale.

The 19-year-old has attracted attention with his performances this season, despite Fulham’s difficult results.

Manchester United are among several clubs monitoring his progress, alongside Liverpool, Arsenal, Borussia Dortmund and Barcelona.

Speaking on the Transfer Insider podcast, Pete O’Rourke explained that King’s growing reputation has put him on the radar of several major clubs.

“Josh King will be on the radar for a number of clubs because of his performances for Fulham so far this season,” O’Rourke said.

“It’s not just Liverpool, there’s been talk of the likes of Manchester United, Arsenal, Borussia Dortmund and even Barcelona looking at Josh King.”

Fulham are not actively planning to let the teenager leave in the near future as he is under contract until 2029. The contract length gives Fulham an upper hand, while interest from multiple clubs indicates a concession is not expected.

“Fulham are not planning for any early departure for Josh King. He is still under contract until 2029, and obviously they’ll want to keep him.”

The reporter also pointed to Fulham’s league position as a factor that could influence their decision. If they become embroiled in a relegation battle, losing one of their best performers would be a major risk.

“If Manchester United or any other club wanted to make an offer, he’s going to be worth in excess of £50million probably right now.”

#2205. Emi Martinez explains Chelsea move as World Cup heartbreak left goalkeeper needing a fresh start

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Emi Martinez has revealed that his move to Chelsea helped him recover mentally from the pain of losing the World Cup final with Argentina.

The goalkeeper left Aston Villa during the summer, with Chelsea confirming a contract until 2029.

He has now explained the personal motivation behind changing clubs.

Speaking after Argentina’s win over Burkina Faso, Martinez discussed his response to the World Cup disappointment in comments reported by TyC Sports.

“I tried changing clubs to refresh myself mentally, and I succeeded.”

Martinez was answering a question about an earlier social-media post that had raised doubts over his Argentina future. He explained that he now felt reinvigorated and ready to continue representing his country.

His explanation gives supporters a clearer understanding of a transfer that formed part of Chelsea’s summer goalkeeping changes.

Those changes also included Filip Jorgensen’s loan move to Strasbourg, as Xabi Alonso reshaped his options.

Martinez’s international break has since brought another positive moment. He received an ovation during the Argentina victory in which Chelsea team-mate Valentin Barco supplied the seventh goal.

#2204. Why must Manchester United move beyond the Glazers?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Glazers took control of Manchester United in 2005. That takeover has since caused so many disruptions and protests by the fans over massive debts and insufficient backing of the team in the transfer market, despite successful periods in the Premier League and Europe.

Now Manchester United need to think about who should control the club in the future. Laurie Whitwell has now shared his views on why Manchester United need an ownership change. He simply wants the Glazers gone from Old Trafford. However, he also recognizes that replacing the current ownership doesn’t automatically mean things will improve for United.

The journalist believes the change in ownership has had a lasting effect on United, with the club still feeling its consequences today. A fresh start might be welcome, but the identity of the next owners would matter just as much as the departure of the existing ones.

“Personally, I would like to see the Glazers gone from Manchester United,” Laurie Whitwell said on The Rest is Football podcast with Gary Lineker.

“That change, you know, just really affected and still impacts United now.”

The desire for different ownership is one thing, but finding people capable of taking the club forward is another.

Whitwell admitted there is no perfect answer when it comes to who should take charge.

That leaves United facing a difficult choice. A sale could offer the chance to change how the club is run, but simply bringing in a new owner would not guarantee better decisions or a brighter future.

Whitwell also raised the idea of United supporters having a greater say in the club’s ownership and decisions, much like the Barcelona model where fans own the club itself. However, he highlighted that the Barcelona model isn’t ideal either and has its own shortcomings.

Changing the names at the top would only be one part of the change. The club would also need an ownership structure capable of making sound decisions and providing a direction.

The issue of ownership goes beyond whether the Glazers should stay or leave. Manchester United needs to find the right people and the right model to secure the club’s future. Getting that decision wrong could leave them facing the same circus the fans are enduring under the Glazers.

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

#2203. Player Ratings: Blackburn 2-1 West Ham as Hermansen Has a Nightmare

West Ham’s promising start at Ewood Park ultimately counted for very little as Scott Parker’s side slipped to a 2-1 defeat against Blackburn Rovers. Despite enjoying plenty of possession and creating opportunities, the Hammers were undone by poor defending and some particularly questionable goalkeeping.

Joël Piroe’s late goal gave West Ham a chance of rescuing something, but the longer the game went on, the worse we seemed to play. There were some decent individual performances, particularly early on, but others will want to forget this afternoon in a hurry.

A really poor afternoon for the goalkeeper. I thought he could have been far more commanding for Blackburn’s opening goal, whether that meant catching the cross, punching it or simply getting something on the ball. He did make a straightforward late save from Todd Cantwell, but that doesn’t change my overall assessment.

One of West Ham’s more reliable performers before being substituted. Made some useful defensive interceptions and regularly pushed forward to support Bowen down the right. Tried a couple of long throws into the penalty area, although they didn’t really cause Blackburn much trouble.

Had some positive moments going forward, including a driving run from defence and a header that went narrowly wide early in the second half. However, I thought he could have done better for Blackburn’s first goal, and his performance deteriorated badly towards the end. Was fortunate not to concede a penalty before slipping twice during a dreadful passage of play that handed Blackburn a dangerous counter-attack.

Probably West Ham’s most dependable defender on the day. Made several important interceptions, particularly down the left where Blackburn repeatedly threatened, and was willing to carry the ball forward to help build attacks. Also produced a decent cross for Castellanos, although the resulting header went wide.

A mixed afternoon for the youngster. I was impressed with his willingness to get forward, his passing combinations with Austbø and some of the crosses he delivered. However, Blackburn repeatedly targeted his side, often leaving him facing two opponents, and he was beaten too easily in the build-up to the opening goal. Still very much learning the position.

Didn’t think he had a particularly good game. Had plenty of involvement, took the corners and got himself into shooting positions, but too often the final product was disappointing. Lost possession unnecessarily on occasions, failed to close down Blackburn’s second goalscorer quickly enough and wasted a promising late free kick with poor delivery.

You certainly couldn’t fault his work rate. At times he seemed to be everywhere, covering at left-back, appearing on the right and getting forward to support the attack. However, he wasn’t nearly as influential on the ball as he had been during the second half against Cardiff, and his shooting was disappointing. Replaced by Castellanos after 64 minutes.

One of West Ham’s more inventive players, particularly during the first half. Looked to move the ball quickly, attempted some clever through passes and wasn’t afraid to shoot, forcing one save and seeing another effort deflected behind. Not everything worked, but at least he was trying to make things happen.

I was genuinely surprised when he was substituted because he’d been one of West Ham’s livelier attacking players. Produced a lovely dribble past two defenders in the first half, linked up well with Mayers and delivered an excellent cross that Bowen headed against the post. Could perhaps have offered Mayers more defensive protection before Blackburn’s opener, but I thought he was contributing plenty going forward.

Blackburn 2-1 West Ham: Austbo was one of the Hammers better players

Worked hard to make things happen and was heavily involved in West Ham’s attacking play, particularly from the right. Unlucky to see his header strike the post following Austbø’s cross, while a difficult chance from close range went wide as he was falling and sliding. Also produced a lovely chipped delivery for Souček’s late header, but couldn’t quite provide the decisive contribution West Ham needed.

A frustrating performance rescued to some extent by an excellent goal. Too often he seemed hesitant in promising positions, allowing defenders to recover, while one particularly poor pass in the second half wasted a good attacking opportunity. However, he reacted brilliantly to Castellanos’ cross in the 82nd minute, firing high into the roof of the net. Whatever else you say about his performance, he took that chance extremely well.

Replaced Austbø after 58 minutes and was practically anonymous. Considering how involved Austbø had been, the substitution certainly didn’t improve West Ham’s attacking threat down the left.

Introduced for Kanté after 64 minutes and provided the cross that led to Piroe’s goal. Also had a header go wide from a Kilman delivery and was involved in the scramble that presented Bowen with a difficult chance. At least contributed something tangible to the attack.

Didn’t have a great deal of time to influence proceedings, particularly with West Ham having already lost their shape. Played one very silly ball into Orford inside our own penalty area, creating unnecessary danger. However, he also got into one of those familiar goalscoring positions in stoppage time, only to direct his header straight at the goalkeeper from Bowen’s excellent delivery.

Another substitute who came into a team that had lost its shape and was struggling for confidence. Was put in a difficult position by Souček’s poor pass inside the penalty area and was fortunate not to concede a penalty. Difficult to judge him too harshly given the limited time he had on the pitch and the state of the game when he entered.

#2202. Chelsea’s £117m Morgan Rogers faced Bournemouth role problem as former scout explains why loan failed

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Morgan Rogers struggled to establish himself at Bournemouth because the role envisaged for him did not fit the team’s usual setup, according to former Cherries scout Des Taylor.

Chelsea’s £117million signing faces his former club on Saturday, with Taylor offering fresh insight into a difficult period in the attacker’s development.

Speaking to BBC Sport’s Nizaar Kinsella in a report syndicated by AOL, Taylor explained:

“We saw him as a number 10, but Parker was not really playing with a number 10.”

Rogers started only three matches and scored once during the loan under Scott Parker.

Taylor highlighted the combination of limited opportunities and appearances outside Rogers’ preferred position, while stressing that he did not blame Parker for the move failing to work.

It adds a specific explanation to the broader setbacks Rogers discussed in our earlier coverage of the experiences that shaped his Chelsea move.

Rogers approaches the reunion after scoring England’s seventh goal against Croatia, finishing after rounding the goalkeeper.

England face Czechia on Tuesday before Chelsea resume their Premier League campaign.

The Blues’ official weekly schedule confirms Bournemouth’s visit for Saturday at 3pm, giving Rogers the opportunity to face the club where his early progress stalled.

#2201. Todd Cantwell reacts as Blackburn Rovers fire West Ham, Scott Parker dig

Scott Parker is already making headlines for all the wrong reasons in charge of West Ham, with the ex-England international having endured a challenging start to proceedings at the London Stadium by failing to win any of his opening two games - and it's causing reaction from both Hammers supporters and opposition players alike.

West Ham only parted company with Nuno Espirito Santo less than a week ago in the wake of successive home defeats against two promotion rivals in West Bromwich Albion and Southampton, which saw the Championship outfit swiftly relieve the long-under-fire Portuguese boss of his duties in favour of sealing a return for Parker.

The 46-year-old had been out of work since leaving Burnley towards the end of the previous campaign, but has wasted no time in getting his feet back under the table and experienced an action-packed debut in the Hammers dugout by claiming a 3-3 draw at Cardiff City last Wednesday after his side had stunningly fallen to a three-goal defecit before the break in South Wales.

West Ham were hot favourites heading into Saturday afternoon's second consecutive away encounter against Blackburn Rovers, although Parker's men would once again flatter to deceive and went two goals down courtesy of strikes from Andri Gudjohnsen and ex-Hammers winger Dapo Afolayan before Joel Piroe's late finish halved the scoreline without setting up yet another dramatic comeback on the road.

Parker's cagey post-match interview at Ewood Park has sparked quite the reaction, too, and after Clarets supporters pointed out just how fatigued and frustrated the former Fulham and Bournemouth boss appeared so early in his return to the club, Blackburn have taken something different from his comments.

Ewood Park isn't exactly renowned for providing an intense and aggressive atmosphere, though Blackburn did not need anything extra to get over the line at home to the pre-season title favourites in what will be remembered as a memorable victory against the odds for Tony Mowbray's side.

That didn't stop Parker from aiming a clear dig towards the East Lancashire outfit, though, remarking in his interview how, on the coach journey to Ewood Park, he "didn't see a Blackburn fan".

Blackburn, however, have been very quick to remind the ex-Burnley figure - hardly a popular presence in this corner of Lancashire in any case, of course - that there were indeed fans present to see his side's winless run continue with a cheeky dig.

The club posted a clip of his quote in a video uploaded to Instagram featuring footage of the Rovers home crowd buoyantly celebrating the hosts' two goals, which was accompanied by a provocative caption reading: "Found some for ya, Scott 😉."

FLW's West Ham fan pundit believes the Hammers should look to offload goalkeeper Mads Hermansen in the January transfer window.

Blackburn are certainly having the last laugh right now and that wasn't limited to the social media admins, with supporters joining the pile-on and some players even getting involved in the antics for good measure.

Namely, club captain Todd Cantwell earned the right to poke fun at the Hammers after enjoying a standout individual display against Parker's side, and the talented playmaker kept it short and sweet by commenting "😂."

His one-emoji dig was followed up by Sam Morsy, who was also in stitches and commented 🤣🤣👏."

Cantwell's comment, unlike that from Morsy, appeared to ignite reaction as some West Ham fans responded to the divisive ex-Norwich City and Rangers starlet, yet he's likely to simply sit back and laugh as his side continue upsetting the odds while ruffling a few feathers in the process.

Blackburn's swipe at Parker was shared via X alongside Instagram, where it gathered an interesting and mixed reaction from both sets of supporters.

West Ham fans, for example, were naturally unimpressed, and this one punter quipped how Rovers' footage failed to capture the high volume of empty seats that we invariably see at Ewood Park.

This fan, meanwhile, is not keen on just how much Blackburn have posted about the victory, suggesting that West Ham's trip up north represented a "cup final" occasion for their more modest Championship counterparts.

Another Hammer banged the drum for humility among those at Ewood, claiming that Parker's quotes had been taken out of context in an effort to gain a cheeky one-up in less-than-humble fashion.

Blackburn fans, by contrast, have been lapping it all up - and who can blame them?

Before the season started, Rovers were widely tipped for relegation whereas Saturday's visitors, fresh off the back of 14 successive seasons in the Premier League with heavy spending prior to an unprecedented relegation last time out, have been backed by the masses to complete a swift top-flight return.

It's not exactly going to plan, though, and Blackburn supporters didn't hold back after their club's jibe, which was described as "unbelievable content" by one fan.

Another was equally happy to have a laugh at Parker's expense, and that can only come as little surprise in light of the manager's aforementioned unpopularity among the Blackburn faithful following his previous exploits with bitter rivals Burnley.

Some have been even more cynical towards Parker, though, and this Blackburn fan labelled the ex-Clarets promotion-winner as "the most annoying manager for about six years now" ahead of cited polarising figures such as Leicester City's Russell Martin and Nathan Jones of Charlton Athletic.

The 7,000-strong West Ham contingent that travelled up to Ewood Park on Saturday returned down south with yet more disappointment as Parker fell short of clinching his first set of three points for the second occasion in under a week.

It's nowhere near good enough for a side with designs on lifting the title with numerous Premier League-quality performers to call upon and, in the last week, the stewardship of a manager who enjoyed a strong playing career with the club before achieving three second-tier promotions in charge of just as many clubs, and he'll be shaking off the jibes and jokes to go back to the drawing board and assess precisely what went wrong.

The encounter itself saw Blackburn score two of their three shots on target, which is hardly a ringing endorsement for goalkeeper Mads Hermansen amid ever-intensifying supporter scrutiny towards the Dane.

West Ham, by contrast, would manage only five shots on target from 21 in total, but with the likes of Jarrod Bowen, Mohamadou Kante and goalscorer Piroe all spurning some glorious opportunities as their side dominated both the possession and chance-creating stakes, the hapless Hammers will scarcely believe their luck riding some of it to overturn a three-goal blitz at Cardiff days earlier.

The two results are hardly representative of an outfit capable of arresting the slide, making up ground and eventually finishing in the top-two, though, and having now fallen out of the play-offs entirely heading into this month's upcoming international break, Parker will need to get it right sooner rather than later at the London Stadium.

Blackburn Rovers and West Ham fans, how do you feel about the dig towards Scott Parker? Who will be having the last laugh at the end of the season? Let us know your thoughts in the comments below!

As for Blackburn, they won't mind one bit and are contrastingly over-performing on their own expectations after returning to winning ways in emphatic fashion against one of the strongest and most well-backed sides they will face all season.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

#2200. Fabrizio Romano shares latest on Martinez contract after explosive rant

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Argentinian defender has also apologized to the United higher ups as well over his comments, as shared by Fabrizio Romano.

Lisandro Martinez has held discussions with Jason Wilcox and Michael Carrick after speaking out following United’s 2-0 defeat to Atletico Madrid.

Martinez had questioned whether Carrick was backed enough in the summer and suggested the club needed changes.

Fabrizio Romano has now confirmed that United consider the matter resolved for now and expect Martinez to remain available for selection.

Speaking about the situation on his YouTube channel, as quoted by Football 365, Romano explained why Martinez’s comments caused concern inside the club.

“He spoke very strongly after the game against Atletico Madrid, which Man Utd lost. It was a very negative moment for the Red Devils. We know that Licha was very strong in his comments. He said that Michael Carrick was not backed enough during the summer transfer window.”

Romano added that Martinez had spoken directly to those at the club after the incident.

“Licha apologised to Man Utd as a club. He spoke to the directors, he spoke to Jason Wilcox, and he spoke to Michael Carrick, who was also involved in this conversation.”

“So, the underlying message remains the same. Lisandro is apologising for the way he expressed himself after a game during a difficult moment for Man Utd, but there was a discussion about the substance of what he said.”

Romano also revealed that United had imposed a financial penalty on the centre-back. However, the punishment does not appear to involve removing him from the squad.

“According to the information available tonight, Lisandro Martinez has been fined.”

He added that Martinez is expected to return to the squad as normal, with the fine considered sufficient punishment for the incident.

“The internal discussion also helped to resolve all the issues. For Man Utd, the case is closed.”

That should bring some immediate clarity to the situation, although questions remain about how the episode could affect Martinez’s longer-term position at Old Trafford.

Romano also pointed towards Martinez’s contract, which expires next summer, with United holding an option to extend it.

“So, let’s see what is going to happen there. For sure, this is the kind of situation that will have an impact on the situation surrounding Lisandro, but we have to see what happens next in terms of future transfers and everything else.”

#2199. Chelsea teenager Reggie Watson captains England and scores as Germany snatch victory with late comeback

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Chelsea midfielder Reggie Watson scored while captaining England Under-17s on Sunday, although Germany’s late comeback ended his international window with a 3-2 defeat.

The 16-year-old equalised from the penalty spot after Oscar Johnson drew a foul inside the area.

According to England’s official match report, Watson beat goalkeeper Nick Hartung with a finish into the left-hand corner.

Mylo Bernard put England ahead on the hour, but Linus Güther equalised seven minutes later. Niko Ilicevic then struck the winner in the 83rd minute.

Barry Lewtas’ side finished the three-match tournament with two victories and one defeat, having previously beaten Israel and Venezuela.

Watson’s involvement offers Chelsea supporters another opportunity to track a teenager already introduced to senior football by Xabi Alonso.

As covered in our report on Watson’s first-team progress, he started the Carabao Cup victory over Leeds United and played the opening 45 minutes.

That appearance, aged 16 years and 226 days, placed him fourth among Chelsea’s youngest competitive debutants.

His England captaincy and goal add a concrete international development to that emerging club story.

#2197. Will Lisandro Martinez play against Leeds after explosive rant?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Lisandro Martinez could return to Manchester United’s starting line-up against Leeds United this weekend, with the defender reportedly available for selection after the controversy surrounding his comments about the club’s summer transfer business.

TEAMtalk transfer insider Graeme Bailey has suggested Michael Carrick could bring the Argentine straight back into the team for the trip to Elland Road. The fixture is expected to be a hostile occasion, making Martinez’s potential involvement an interesting one.

“I am told that Martinez is available for selection this weekend, and there is every chance Michael Carrick starts him,” Bailey said.

The defender recently found himself in trouble after questioning whether Manchester United had done enough in the summer transfer window. He apologised to the club’s hierarchy and was fined, but the incident has not apparently created a divide between him and the manager.

Bailey also dismissed suggestions that Martinez’s comments were an attack on Michael Carrick, insisting the defender and his teammates continue to support the head coach.

“Martinez was not critical of Carrick – far from it. This isn’t a repeat of something like Mo Salah criticising Arne Slot last season. It is almost the opposite.”

According to Bailey, the United dressing room remains firmly behind Carrick despite the team’s disappointing start to the campaign. Senior players reportedly believe the current manager is still the right person to lead the team, rather than bringing in another face to replace him.

“Martinez and the squad are 100% behind Carrick. They know there is pressure after a poor start to the season, but they do not want to see a change.”

Bailey went on to suggest that the fallout may have strengthened the bond between the players and their manager.

“Martinez and the senior players firmly believe nobody can come in and do a better job than Carrick. If anything, this has brought the group closer together.”

#2196. Chelsea’s Romeo Lavia unlocks France with brilliant assist before Belgium suffer FOUR-goal late collapse

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Romeo Lavia produced a superb defence-splitting assist for Belgium against France on Monday, although a late collapse left the Chelsea midfielder facing a 4-1 defeat.

Lavia found Dodi Lukebakio with a forward pass from inside his own half, sending the attacker through to score in the 35th minute.

Sofascore’s match record credits Lavia with the assist, rewarding a moment of vision that briefly put Belgium on course for victory in Paris.

The Chelsea midfielder combined that attacking contribution with two interceptions and two tackles won during his 87 minutes.

Lavia started after suspensions ruled Kevin De Bruyne and Youri Tielemans out of Belgium’s midfield.

His assist demonstrated how quickly he can turn possession deep in midfield into a scoring opportunity with one forward pass.

However, Belgium could not protect their advantage. France’s late response left the visitors with a heavy defeat despite leading for much of the match.

Lavia now turns his attention back to Chelsea, who resume their Premier League campaign at home to Bournemouth on Saturday.

#2195. Dave Challinor's private Sheffield Wednesday notes exposed during Oldham defeat

Dave Challinor's debut as Sheffield Wednesday manager didn't go to plan, as the Owls were dumped out of the FA Cup by League Two opposition in Oldham Athletic, and there were parts of the performance he was especially angered by.

Oldham were good value for their win after extra time, with Wednesday putting in a tired performance that not many were expecting from Challinor's first game in charge. It's the first time that the 51-year-old has opened up a dugout role without a win.

The first half, especially, wasn't good enough by the new manager's standards. Wednesday dominated possession, but only had one shot and just three touches in the Latics' box. It was quite reminiscent of what angered Owls fans about Henrik Pedersen's setup.

Challinor wasn't going to let this become the norm, though, and was seen writing plenty of notes in his notebook for what was going to be quite the angry team talk at the break.

Oldham's win over Wednesday was shown live on TNT Sports on Sunday evening, and with the TV cameras out, the Owls were unable to put on a show in the first half of Dave Challinor's reign.

Collect every point to win the round. Don’t let Neil Warnock catch you.

Arrow keys or WASD to move. P to pause, M to mute.

Swipe on the pitch or use the pad to move.

The cameras caught an over-the-shoulder shot of the Wednesday boss making some notes midway through the first half, and he'd already had a full page's worth of grievances.

Amongst some more expletive expressions to describe parts of that first half, there were the words "WEAK," "compete !!" and "Stupid !"

Immediately, Wednesday fans took to X to try and see if anyone could make out what was being written by their new boss midway through the first half, which led to many screenshots, identical to the one above, being tweeted.

"Not a happy boy. Quite rightly," one Wednesday fan said in response to those notes being relayed on X during the game. "Hahahahhahaa that’s class. He’s impressed I see," another commented.

"This is hilarious btw. It took him 2 minutes to see how dire Palmer is," was also among the comments, as was "That result might just be what Challinor needed to witness."

Sheffield Wednesday fans soured very quickly on Henrik Pedersen when results weren't going their way, but despite bowing out of the FA Cup at the first hurdle to a side a division below, the majority of reaction during and after the game was directed at the performance on the pitch, rather than in the dugout.

After the game, Challinor continued to cut a frustrated figure, with the majority of fans agreeing with what he was saying, with the overarching feeling that some of the players aren't where they should be physically at this stage in the campaign.

Do you agree with Dave Challinor's assessment of his first game as Sheffield Wednesday boss? Let us know in the comments.

Most Wednesday supporters felt disappointed with how Pedersen would protect players after poor results, whereas the new boss seems content with outlining where things aren't good enough, which in turn is winning fans over.

While it would have been an ideal scenario for Dave Challinor to walk in, tweak one or two things and Wednesday go from strength to strength, the first 45 minutes of his tenure in charge highlight that this definitely won't be the case.

Challinor is coming into a side that, by his own admission, isn't there physically and is struggling massively with injury.

Mason Burstow is the latest casualty, while Ashley Maynard-Brewer was forced off during the game and Harry Gray was removed from the squad at the last minute after failing to get through the warm-up.

Football League World assesses and ranks the FIFTEEN best away fanbases in the EFL right now

The 51-year-old, evidently, is going to need time, but it feels that the Wednesday fanbase, or at least the most vocal parts, are happy to give him that. At least, more so than they were with Challinor's predecessor.

To many, Challinor was the number one option to take Wednesday forward at the start of the season, and now he's in post; those in the stands at Hillsborough are willing to give him enough time to take them forward. In the meantime, his ruthless nature is helping them get onside, even if the results take some time to follow suit.

#2194. West Ham Go Full Circle as Blackburn Expose Familiar Problems

West Ham’s 2-1 defeat against Blackburn Rovers was a particularly frustrating result because, for large periods of the game, Scott Parker’s side appeared to be the better team. We started brightly, enjoyed plenty of possession and created opportunities, yet somehow managed to leave Ewood Park with nothing.

What concerns me most isn’t necessarily the result itself, although that’s bad enough. It’s the manner in which West Ham gradually deteriorated as the afternoon progressed. Even after Joël Piroe pulled a goal back late on, I thought we looked increasingly disorganised and short of confidence.

And that’s perhaps the strangest aspect of Parker’s first two matches. We started against Cardiff looking completely devoid of confidence, rediscovered it during that extraordinary second-half comeback, began confidently against Blackburn and somehow finished looking just as nervous as we had before.

I was actually quite encouraged by the opening stages. West Ham pressed high, moved the ball around reasonably well and looked determined to take the game to Blackburn. Max Kilman was particularly proactive, stepping out of defence to offer an additional passing option, while Divine Mukasa and Arne Engels were regularly involved in midfield.

Down the left, Ezra Mayers and Edvin Austbø developed a promising partnership. Mayers was willing to get forward and Austbø looked lively, producing one lovely piece of skill to beat two Blackburn players before delivering a dangerous cross.

Blackburn 2-1 West Ham: Austbo was one of the Hammers better players

However, there was a problem developing on that side of the pitch. Blackburn repeatedly managed to isolate Mayers against two opponents, and while the youngster was doing some good things offensively, he wasn’t receiving enough protection defensively.

Joël Piroe was also struggling to make the most of promising situations. On one occasion, he found himself with only a single defender to beat but hesitated for so long that three opponents eventually surrounded him. West Ham were having plenty of the ball, but Blackburn were defending deeply and making it difficult to create clear opportunities.

The second half initially offered more encouragement. Mavropanos headed narrowly wide from an Engels corner, Piroe had an effort saved and then Austbø produced an excellent cross which Bowen headed against the post. At that stage, West Ham were piling on the pressure and Blackburn were struggling to get out of their own half.

Then came the sucker punch. In the 54th minute, Blackburn broke down their right, with Mayers unable to stop the delivery into the penalty area. Andri Guðjohnsen converted, but I thought several West Ham players could have done better.

Austbø might have provided more protection for Mayers, Mavropanos could have defended the situation better and I certainly felt Mads Hermansen should have been more commanding. Whether he caught the cross, punched it or simply got something on the ball, I thought the goalkeeper had an opportunity to intervene.

It was a horrible goal to concede, particularly given how much of the attacking West Ham had been doing. Parker responded by introducing Manor Solomon for Austbø, a substitution I found surprising. The Norwegian had been one of our more involved attacking players, while Solomon subsequently struggled to make any impression.

Mohamadou Kanté was then replaced by Taty Castellanos, but West Ham were finding it increasingly difficult to establish the same attacking rhythm. Blackburn’s second goal arrived in the 71st minute, with Afolayan producing an excellent finish.

Kilman had gone down following contact to his face, but too many West Ham players appeared more interested in appealing for a foul than dealing with the danger. Engels, in particular, could have done more to close down the shot. Whatever the circumstances, you cannot simply stop defending because you believe the referee should have awarded a free kick.

At 2-0, the game appeared to be slipping away, but West Ham eventually found a route back through something remarkably straightforward. Castellanos got down the right-hand side and delivered a dangerous ball into the penalty area. Piroe reacted quickly and fired high into the roof of the net in the 82nd minute.

It was an excellent finish from a striker who, in truth, hadn’t been having a particularly good afternoon. But sometimes football really is that simple. Get the ball wide, get down the line and deliver something dangerous into the box. Give your centre-forward an opportunity to attack the ball.

Suddenly it was Blackburn 2-1 West Ham, with enough time remaining to rescue a point. Unfortunately, the expected late onslaught never really materialised. In fact, I thought West Ham became progressively worse.

Mavropanos endured a dreadful few minutes, fortunate not to concede a penalty before slipping and gifting Blackburn a counter-attack. He then slipped again while attempting to recover, while Souček subsequently played a very poor pass into Lewis Orford inside our own penalty area, creating another potentially disastrous situation.

West Ham looked increasingly nervous, and the defensive organisation seemed to disappear. There were still opportunities at both ends in stoppage time, with Todd Cantwell shooting straight at Hermansen when Blackburn might have made it 3-1.

Souček then headed directly at the Blackburn goalkeeper following an excellent chipped delivery from Bowen. It was precisely the sort of position from which we’ve seen Souček score numerous times, but once again West Ham had failed to make the most of a promising opportunity.

I keep coming back to the confidence issue because I think it explains an awful lot about what we’ve witnessed over these two matches. Against Cardiff, West Ham were absolutely dreadful during the opening 45 minutes, before producing a transformation after half-time that was almost impossible to believe.

Suddenly the players looked confident, aggressive and capable of playing some very good football. That improvement appeared to carry into the opening stages at Ewood Park, where we pressed Blackburn, moved the ball with purpose and looked considerably more comfortable in possession.

Yet by the closing stages, the confidence had completely evaporated. It was almost as though we’d gone full circle in the space of two matches, ending up precisely where we started. That is particularly disappointing because I still believe this West Ham squad possesses considerably more ability than recent performances suggest.

There were some positives. Mukasa showed invention, Austbø looked dangerous, Kilman had a decent game and Piroe took his goal extremely well. But the defensive mistakes, poor decision-making and increasingly nervous football were all too familiar.

I don’t think Scott Parker can be held responsible for everything we’ve witnessed this week. He’s barely had any meaningful time on the training pitch, and the international break offers him an opportunity to start properly implementing his ideas.

However, the evidence of these two games suggests that restoring confidence isn’t simply a matter of delivering one rousing team talk. The psychological side of West Ham’s problems may be every bit as important as the tactical side.

The frustrating thing is that we’ve already seen how much better these players can perform when they believe in themselves. Against Blackburn, we watched that belief gradually disappear again.

Kris Gonzo (commonly known to the West Ham community as Gonzo) is the co-founder of Hammers Chat (established 2014) and the lead video content creator and columnist for Claret and Hugh.

Leeds United welcome Manchester United to Elland Road on Sunday, with both sides looking to put a difficult run of results behind them and get their Premier League season on track.

Leeds United suffered a 2-1 defeat away to Arsenal last weekend despite James Justin scoring to put them in front in the 55th minute.

Manchester United also dropped points in the Premier League, drawing 1-1 with Tottenham, before losing 2-0 to Atletico Madrid in the Champions League away from home.

Michael Carrick’s side have struggled to find consistency in recent weeks and have come under immense pressure to get in results. The Whites meanwhile will hope that home advantage can make the difference when United face their bitter rivals on Elland Road.

Leeds have recorded one win, two draws and two defeats in their last five matches in all competitions. Their best result came against Newcastle, where they won, but a 6-3 defeat to Chelsea in the Carabao Cup and the loss to Arsenal have left them searching for a response.

Manchester United have also won just once in their last five matches. Their 4-0 Champions League victory over Sabah FK was the only comfortable win, but defeats to Manchester City and Atletico Madrid, alongside draws against Fulham and Tottenham, have raised questions about their form and Michael Carrick.

Leeds are waiting on Dan James, who returned from international duty with a back problem and missed the Arsenal game. There is better news regarding Matthijs de Ligt, who could return to contention. Leny Yoro is also available for selection despite his Champions League suspension.

The match takes place at Elland Road on Sunday, October 18. Kick-off is scheduled for 2 pm BST (1pm GMT).

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Estêvão and Jorrel Hato have reached the final shortlist for the 2026 Golden Boy award, giving Chelsea two contenders.

The organisers’ official announcement confirms their inclusion among Europe’s leading young players.

Arsenal’s Myles Lewis-Skelly, Barcelona defender Pau Cubarsí and Bayern Munich’s Lennart Karl also feature.

Barcelona winger Lamine Yamal is absent because the rules prevent previous winners from claiming the award again.

An international panel of 50 journalists will select the winner, with the announcement scheduled for November.

Estêvão’s nomination follows a productive finish to his international window. He scored and supplied an assist in Brazil’s 4-0 victory over India on Saturday.

The winger now returns to Chelsea with those contributions behind him as he seeks more opportunities under Xabi Alonso.

Hato provides a different profile among Chelsea’s nominees. The Dutch defender can operate at centre-back or on the left, with his versatility offering Alonso options alongside Pep Chavarria.

Both remain in contention for the individual honour, although nomination alone does not establish either as the favourite.

#2191. Willian admits Chelsea exit was a ‘big mistake’ after wanting Arsenal escape just THREE months later

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Willian has described leaving Chelsea for Arsenal as a mistake, explaining how quickly his enthusiasm for the move turned into regret.

Speaking to former team-mate John Obi Mikel on The Obi One Podcast, the Brazilian revisited the decision that ended his seven-year Stamford Bridge spell in 2020.

Chelsea offered two more years, while Willian wanted three. His contract-length disagreement with Chelsea featured in ReadChelsea’s coverage at the time.

Arsenal offered the longer deal. Willian also valued keeping his family in London and says Mikel Arteta outlined a central role for him.

“To be honest I should have stayed,” he said, in comments reported by the Mirror.

The winger followed the same route as Petr Cech, who explained his own Chelsea-to-Arsenal move through the desire for regular football.

Willian says the reality differed from the picture presented during talks. Within three months, he had told his agent he wanted to leave.

He linked that frustration to his performances, explaining that unhappiness prevented him from producing his best football.

His Arsenal spell ultimately lasted one season. He scored once in 37 appearances before leaving for Corinthians in 2021.

#2189. Kim Hellberg footage after Middlesbrough's draw v Watford is becoming a talking point

Middlesbrough dropped points and failed to score for the second time this week on Saturday at Watford, and Boro boss Kim Hellberg was seen visibly frustrated after the final whistle.

The draw at Vicarage Road saw Middlesbrough relinquish the top spot in the Championship ahead of the November international break, with Birmingham City climbing above them on goal difference.

It's not how Boro would have wanted to sign off this mini block of fixtures between international breaks, and the performance against Watford, especially, would have provided plenty of food for thought in the dugout.

Pick any two players. Send the code, and your mate enters it here to play the same pair.

Indeed, this was the second successive game without leading talisman and Championship top scorer Will Lankshear due to injury, and it's almost universally agreed that if the 21-year-old was leading the line on Saturday, they'd have cruised to victory, as Boro had 18 shots, 34 touches in Watford's box and four big chances, but couldn't score.

Middlesbrough boss Kim Hellberg has shown how passionate he is about the game in the 12 months he's spent in charge at the Riverside. Therefore, it was clear that a result like this, following such a dominant performance, was going to affect him.

After the game, the Swede was filmed staring blankly at the ground for around half a minute, just reflecting on the game, and Boro fans have reacted accordingly.

Some have made jokey comments about the state of the frontline when Lankshear is injured. One Boro fan took it upon themselves to caption the video, "What having Kyle Joseph as an attacking option does to man." Another commented, "Please god make Will be fit, please god make Will be fit…..🙏."

"He’s got Kyle Joseph depression," was also among the reactions to the video.

Football League World has asked AI to rank the top nine toughest fanbases in the EFL Championship right now from least to most

The majority of comments in response to Hellberg's post-match reaction were about how invested he is in the game, though, and Middlesbrough fans know how lucky they are to have the Swede in charge.

"You can just tell this isn’t performative. Like the fist bumps and the celebrations when we win, or the tears during the play-off debacle last season, Kim ‘gets it’, he’s immersed himself into the #Boro way. Never seen a manager so invested in us," one Boro fan tweeted.

"Carries it all, the highs and lows just like the fans. “A good leader leads the people from above them. A great leader leads the people from within them," was also among the comments, as was, "The reaction of a man who puts the weight of the world on his own shoulders #UTB."

"Very powerful, but also makes me want to give him a hug and tell him he's one of my favourite ever Boro gaffers and everything will be alright," someone commented in response to the video, showcasing the love and respect Boro fans have towards their head coach.

This is the reaction of a man spent after a disappointing result, having coached what was effectively the perfect game.

Plenty of head coaches nowadays are focused on the journey, the performance levels and good underlying numbers, and Hellberg is definitely one of those, but he also recognises how important it is to win games, too.

As one Boro fan mentioned, he carries the weight of expectation amongst Middlesbrough fans, who are desperate to return to the Premier League after a decade away, and you can imagine he takes not winning as seriously as some of those in the stands do, too.

It's reactions like this that are why many are touting Hellberg to become a top-flight manager in the near future, and Boro supporters hope it'll be with them.

However, it's hard to ignore the fact that, while Lankshear sits on the sidelines, Middlesbrough struggles to score goals and win games.

Boro's wasteful nature in front of goal dominated the narrative surrounding them in the final few months of last season as they slipped out of the running for automatic promotion and into the play-offs, and they could have been out of sight in the first half of their play-off semi-final first leg against Southampton if not for poor finishing, too.

Lankshear has papered over those cracks, as he's been brilliant at finishing the plenty of chances Middlesbrough create in a game, but the alarm bells are starting to ring without him.

Do you think Middlesbrough can turn their finishing troubles around without Will Lankshear in the side? Let us know in the comments.

Hopefully, the 21-year-old will be back after the international break and won't suffer any more injury setbacks, as that could be the difference between promotion and some grim de ja vu.

It's something Hellberg will be mulling over for the next two weeks, and it seemingly started immediately after the final whistle at Watford.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

#2188. Jamie Carragher names Man Utd Star who impressed him against Leeds

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United had a difficult evening once again as they were held to a 1-1 draw at Leeds United. Pressure mounts on Michael Carrick as United have just 7 points from 7 games.

Manchester United took the lead through Bryan Mbeumo in the first half, with the forward finishing Youri Tielemans’ excellent cross from the right. However, the visitors couldn’t hold on to their advantage as Dominic Calvert-Lewin equalised for Leeds United from a corner.

The draw extended United’s frustrating run of results, leaving Michael Carrick still searching for the consistency his side need.

Carragher, though, believes there were positives to take from the trip, particularly one performance at the back.

Jamie Carragher has singled out Harry Maguire for praise after Manchester United drew 1-1 with Leeds United at Elland Road on Sunday.

Speaking on Sky Sports, the former Liverpool defender admitted that picking up a point at Elland Road was not necessarily a bad result in isolation, even if United’s recent form made it feel like a missed opportunity.

“Going away to Leeds is going to be tricky for any team except for Manchester City and Arsenal at the moment,” Carragher said.

“A lot of teams will take a point at Leeds this season but the problem for Manchester United is the results have been so poor that they need to start winning games so it almost feels like two points dropped.”

Carragher then turned his attention to Maguire, who he felt played a major role in keeping United in the game when Leeds began to push for a winner.

“But in isolation, getting a point at Leeds is a decent result. I thought Harry Maguire was fantastic, especially in the second half.”

Leeds United grew into the contest after Calvert-Lewin brought them level, putting United under pressure during the closing stages. Carragher felt Maguire’s efforts in defence helped prevent the visitors from falling apart.

“After Leeds got that goal back there was a stage of the game where it felt like Man United could go under, but Maguire at the back held it together for them. He was fantastic.”

The result may feel like another opportunity missed for Carrick, but Maguire’s performance gave United something positive to take away from Elland Road.

#2187. Arsenal transfer news: Bournemouth make Eli Junior Kroupi decision ahead of January window

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal have been dealt a potential setback in their pursuit of Bournemouth forward Eli Junior Kroupi. The Cherries are reportedly refusing to consider a January sale.

The Gunners are not alone in keeping an eye on the 20-year-old after he returned from injury. Manchester United have also made enquiries, and Tottenham remains a strong possibility after chasing him over the summer.

However, Bournemouth are keen to keep Kroupi at the Vitality Stadium following the January transfer window. His return will provide new head coach Marco Rose with another attacking threat. Helping to improve their position in the Premier League.

This could mean that Arsenal will have to plan a long-term pursuit of the player.

Kroupi provided a positive impression in his debut season in English football. Becoming one of the most exciting young forwards in the Premier League.

Overall, he hit the target on 13 occasions in the previous league campaign. Attracting the attention of a number of top European clubs.

Paris Saint Germain, Barcelona, Chelsea and Tottenham were among the clubs that were said to keep following his progress over the summer months.

However, he was unable to make the move after suffering an injury to his foot which required surgery. Kroupi has now returned to action. He came on as a late substitute in Bournemouth’s 3-0 victory against Manchester United last weekend.

Arsenal have again been linked. The Gunners are now known to be one of a number of clubs to have asked about the player’s injury and fitness ahead of improving their attacking options.

Tottenham are also interested, having had previous interest in signing him. There is no real suggestion an offer has been made.

Bournemouth are the main problem for Arsenal, with Kroupa said to be unwilling to leave the Cherries in January. He is seen as an important asset for them in the second half of the season.

However, Rose’s side has had a difficult start to the season so far. Having him at his disposal could prove to be vital. The forward can bring goals and quality into the attack. Bournemouth believes it will help change their fortunes in the coming months.

They will also look to keep their attacking unit intact. Players such as Rayan and Marcus Tavernier are ones that Bournemouth will look to keep hold of. Alex Scott may also catch the eye of other clubs ahead of the New Year.

Bournemouth will be desperate to avoid losing a number of key players in one transfer window.

That stance leaves Arsenal facing a difficult decision if they want to pursue Kroupi. The Gunners can continue monitoring his performances and fitness, but a January move currently looks unlikely.

The forward’s return to regular football will give interested clubs another opportunity to assess his progress. Arsenal, Tottenham and Manchester United could therefore remain attentive to developments as the season continues.

Bournemouth are currently in the best position and Arsenal might have to wait until the summer to see how the Cherries will hold up when faced with losing one of their most promising young players.

#2184. Como vs Man Utd Preview: Team news, injuries, form and where to watch

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United travel to Italy on Wednesday, looking for their first win since September 10, when they face Como in the Champions League.

Michael Carrick’s side have struggled to build any momentum this season. Sunday’s 1-1 draw against Leeds extended their winless run to six matches in all competitions, leaving United with just seven points from their opening seven Premier League games.

The pressure on Carrick continues to grow. A win in Europe would offer some relief, but Como will not make life easy for Manchester United.

Manchester United began their Champions League campaign with a convincing 4-0 win over Sabah FK but lost 2-0 to Atletico Madrid in their next match. They have since drawn with Leeds, having also failed to beat Fulham and Tottenham in the Premier League.

Their last six matches across all competitions have produced three draws and three defeats. Michael Carrick is in pressure of his own and needs a response from his players, particularly with United sitting 13th in the Premier League and 13th in the Champions League standings.

Como have shown more promise. Cesc Fabregas’ side beat RB Leipzig 4-1 in Europe before losing 3-2 to Feyenoord. They returned to winning ways against Fiorentina, claiming a 3-1 victory that moved them up to fifth in Serie A, with 14 points from seven games.

Como have scored 11 goals and conceded eight across their last six matches in all competitions. United have struggled, with their only clean sheet of the season coming against Sabah FK.

United have several confirmed absentees ahead of the trip to Italy. Patrick Dorgu, Manuel Ugarte and Benjamin Sesko are ruled out, while Leny Yoro is suspended for the European fixture after picking a red against Atletico Madrid.

Lisandro Martinez is a major concern after coming off at half-time against Leeds with a thigh problem. Ayden Heaven, Luke Shaw and Kobbie Mainoo are all doubtful for the game.

Como will be without Assane Diao, who is sidelined with a thigh injury. Moise Kean has a shin problem and is a doubt, while Trevoh Chalobah is also a fitness concern with a muscular issue.

The match takes place at the Stadio Giuseppe Sinigaglia on Wednesday, October 21, with kick-off at 5:45 pm BST.

UK viewers can watch the Champions League fixture live on TNT Sports 2 and HBO Max.

#2183. West Ham face Taty Castellanos, River Plate transfer exit threat

As West Ham continue to fall short of rekindling their fortunes despite Scott Parker's recent appointment, the possibility of Taty Castellanos departing the London Stadium in the coming months has now emerged in a development that will spark mixed reactions from Hammers supporters.

West Ham's return to the Championship following 14 successive seasons of solid Premier League standing is not going to plan right now, with the East Londoners appearing increasingly unlikely to seal a previously-expected top-flight return at the very first time of asking as champions.

A mixed run of form featuring consecutive home defeats at home to a pair of promotion rivals in Southampton and West Bromwich Albion ultimately ended Nuno Espirito Santo's unpopular tenure in charge, and the Portuguese boss has been swiftly succeeded by former Hammers midfielder Parker.

The ex-Bournemouth and Fulham boss, having already achieved three promotions from the second-tier with as many clubs after most recently guiding Burnley back to the promised land in 2025 before his dismissal towards the end of the previous campaign, has encountered a challenging return to the club thus far, though, taking just one point from his opening two matches.

Last Wednesday's thrilling 3-3 comeback draw at Cardiff City after overturning a three-goal defecit at half-time was followed up by a disappointing 2-1 defeat away to Blackburn Rovers over the weekend, and with the Hammers yet to enjoy the anticipated upturn under Parker, they now face the threat of losing one of their big-name performers once the January window rolls around.

Castellanos has endured a particularly difficult and underwhelming introduction to the Championship, which the Argentine forward was expected to light up after his mid-season arrival back in January preceded encouraging goalscoring form in spite of his side's ultimate failure to dodge the drop.

Six goals from 18 Premier League appearances means that the current return of just one strike across 14 second-tier fixtures - nine of which have been starts - is not what fans were anticipating, and they had presented vocal criticism towards the divisive 28-year-old for a number of weeks before Parker hauled the attacker off at the interval against the Bluebirds and then dropped his name out of the starting line-up at Ewood Park.

It has now emerged that Castellanos could leave East London as early as January, with a fresh report from Argentine-based outlet El Intransigente claiming strong long-term interest from River Plate.

As per the update, River Plate have held interest in the two-cap Argentine international's services in recent windows and he represents a "top target" in the eyes of manager Leonardo Ponzio.

Blackburn Rovers captain Todd Cantwell has been among those reacting to the club's dig towards Scott Parker's West Ham after Saturday's 2-1 victory

It's said that the Buenos Aries-based giants feel strongly about Castellano's profile and international experience, with the striker having risen to prominence outside of his native Argentina in Uruguay with Montevideo City Torque before enjoying productive spells at New York City, Girona and Lazio ahead of joining West Ham earlier this year for a reported £26 million figure on a four-and-a-half year contract.

West Ham supporters have grown increasingly critical of Castellanos and his long-term future under Parker remains unclear at this moment in time, although a fresh hurdle could prevent River Plate from sealing the deal.

River Plate have been presented with a significant obstacle to overcome if they intend on returning Castellanos to his homeland, as it's a move which wouldn't come cheaply for the 38-time Primera Division champions.

Indeed, the report has added that the salary Castellanos earns at the London Stadium is the main challenge for River Plate right now, leaving the club to weigh up alternative options such as acquiring the sought-after frontman on loan or even purchasing a percentage of his rights.

Both of those options, of course, would require the prior approval of West Ham, whose own stance on potentially parting ways with the ex-Lazio striker just a year after his arrival with a club likely unable to recoup the figure of purchase is also not yet known.

It's noted that Castellanos' lengthy contract with the Championship promotion hopefuls puts Parker's side in a comfortable negotiating decision, and hold all the cards as River Plate will not be able to wrap up any deal without their support.

Interestingly, however, the update also makes reference to previous interest elsewhere, reminding that Castellanos had reportedly wished to leave West Ham in the summer amid loan interest from Seville-based rivals Real Betis and Sevilla that never materialised.

The London Stadium faithful remain very much divided on Castellanos at the moment, with some making their minds up on the out-of-form forward and others willing to persist with further patience.

Prior claims of a January exit if the price is met at the end of the previous month saw some West Ham fans refuse to hold back on their feelings about Castellanos' future, and this one fan believes that his hapless form in the Championship would make for no great loss if he was to depart.

Another would be happy to see the Hammers striker leave at any cost, even if the club themselves surely won't take that stance.

Castellanos has left some frustrated for reasons beyond his inability to find the back of the net on a regular basis, too, and having formerly scored at a relatively-prolific rate leading the line for a side relegated from the Premier League, his work-rate and application have been called into question.

His display in the aforementioned draw at Cardiff drew particular criticism, and West Ham were a much more fluid, functional and threatening outfit once the Argentine was withdrawn for Joel Piroe at the break.

Now, with the Suriname international scoring in successive encounters, it's unclear how Castellanos will force his way back into starting contention ahead of a positional rival with a historically-goal-laden hit-rate at this level.

West Ham fans, how do you feel about Taty Castellanos' future? Would you hold onto the striker, or is it time to cash in? What should his valuation be? Let us know your thoughts in the comments below!

But there are other fans who continue to stand by Castellanos, and considering how he fared at the highest level last time out, it's no great surprise that certain supporters still believe he can come good in the second-tier.

Either way, there are plenty of West Ham fans who, despite the criticism of the majority, do feel that the divisive striker would thrive in a system that emphasises chance creation under a manager willing to trust in his services and get the best out of an undeniably-talented striker with pedigree in more prestigious and higher-quality divisions.

This punter, meanwhile, reckons that cashing in on Castellanos would be a "stupid decision", but only time will tell to see what action West Ham decide to take and whether a deal can successfully be thrashed out with River Plate.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

#2182. Arsenal could expose Liverpool’s biggest weakness at Anfield

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal have spent years struggling to impose themselves at Anfield. However, Mikel Arteta could have an opportunity to change that record when his side face Liverpool on Sunday.

The Gunners have often struggled against the physicality of Liverpool on Merseyside. Since Jurgen Klopp took charge, Liverpool have built a reputation for being high pressing, fast, and making their opponents make mistakes.

However, looking at the latest stats, it seems that Liverpool are no longer as physical, meaning that Arsenal could target their weaknesses.

The disparity is stark between the two teams, with Liverpool having run the least distance of any Premier League side this season. Meanwhile, Arsenal are running the most.

If Arteta’s side can capitalise on this and create fast attacks from possession, then they can utilise this to their advantage.

Liverpool have shifted away from their high-octane style associated with much of Klopp’s reign. Arne Slot has slowly moved towards a more possession-based approach, focusing on keeping the ball.

Andoni Iraola looks keen to add more intensity to their pressing. The Reds have had some of the best pressing stats in the Premier League this season (Opta Analyst).

However, they are not getting the desired intensity from the system.

In addition to chasing the opposition when they have the ball, players must quickly recover, cut off passing lanes and return to their defensive positions.

This is where Arsenal might have an opportunity. If they can get past Liverpool’s first line of pressure, they may have space to push forward before the home side can get back into position.

Another area that the Gunners could target is Liverpool’s midfield.

Alexis MacAllister and Ryan Gravenberch are technically gifted and very composed when they have the ball. But neither are a true defensive midfielder, which raises questions over their ability to stop teams playing through the middle.

Arsenal have the players to exploit this. Declan Rice can put in an energetic, physical performance and drive the play forward. Myles Lewis-Skelly provides another option who can run with the ball into dangerous areas.

This can cause problems for the Liverpool midfield. Arsenal can try to disrupt Mac Allister and Gravenberch, not letting them set the tempo of the game.

If they win these individual battles, it can stop Liverpool from getting the ball control they want.

The task is certainly a tall one. Arsenal have lost their last 16 games at Anfield, including seven in Arteta’s reign.

Whilst that may be the case, the current Liverpool team are very different to the one Arsenal have faced on a regular basis during Klopp’s time in charge.

They certainly can’t expect to be outplayed, but they must take advantage of Liverpool’s failings.

Arsenal are currently second in the Premier League with 21 points from eight games. They will want to win here but will need to do more than just run them into the ground.

If Rice and Lewis-Skelly can push forward and pressurise them, forcing them to make mistakes, then Arteta could finally have his winning recipe at Anfield.

#2180. Rio Ferdinand disappointed Man Utd didn’t sign midfielder better than Elliot Anderson

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Rio Ferdinand has admitted he is disappointed that Manchester United failed to sign Ayyoub Bouaddi, with the highly-rated midfielder now making an impression at Manchester City.

Manchester City beat several rivals to secure Bouaddi’s signature over the summer in a deal worth £86 million. The Morocco international became the Premier League’s most expensive teenager, and Rio Ferdinand believes Manchester United missed out on a special talent.

The former United defender was particularly impressed by Bouaddi’s second-half cameo during City’s Champions League defeat to Paris Saint-Germain last week. His performance reminded Rio Ferdinand why he had been talking about the youngster throughout the previous year and during the summer transfer window.

Speaking on his YouTube channel, Ferdinand highlighted how Bouaddi was the highlight of the game against PSG and who they missed.

“He came on and showed PSG that they may have missed out on someone who was on their doorstep when he was playing in France,” Ferdinand said.

The former England international also confirmed that Manchester United had been interested in bringing the midfielder to Old Trafford, but the transfer fee was too expensive.

“Manchester United were in for him, 100%, but the fee was just too much,” he added.

Ferdinand was left frustrated by what he saw from the teenager, who turned 19 this month.

“Just that little cameo against PSG makes you go, ‘oh, I can’t believe we missed out on that. I can’t believe it.’ I’m so gutted, so gutted. He’s bad! He’s so calm under pressure.”

Rio Ferdinand suggested Bouaddi’s quality could soon make the midfield position in a packed Manchester City his regular role.

City already have Elliot Anderson and Enzo Fernandez among their midfield options, but Ferdinand believes Bouaddi has the ability to stand out alongside them.

“They’ve got Elliot Anderson and Enzo Fernandez in that midfield as well but this kid could be the best of the three, easily.”
