#2178. Arsenal news: Arsenal vs Liverpool. Arteta targets Anfield record as Liverpool prepare for Premier League clash

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal vs Liverpool: Arsenal travel to Anfield on Sunday looking to maintain their strong start to the Premier League season and end a long wait for victory at Liverpool’s home ground.

A blistering start to the season has seen Mikel Arteta’s side win seven of their first eight league games, leaving them tied on points with Manchester City at the top of the table and six points ahead of Liverpool going into the game.

However, the Gunners have not beaten Liverpool in the Premier League at Anfield since 2012/13. Arteta will be aware that the Reds are starting to gel under Andoni Iraola.

They have won three of their last four league games after a lacklustre start and had their first league win at home this season with their 2-0 win over Brighton.

After replacing Arne Slot over the summer, Iraola brought his impressive three-year spell at Bournemouth to Anfield. However, Liverpool have struggled to adjust to the new manager, losing 3 of their first 4 league games.

But they have recently improved and are looking to play a much faster style of football. As Adrian Clarke notes, they are pressing more, not sitting back as much and wanting to attack quickly when they win the ball.

They have been the most direct attackers in the Premier League. They have pace to spare within their attack, with players like Alexander Isak and Cody Gakpo providing a threat in advanced areas.

Isak comes into the game in good form after finding the back of the net six times in his first eight league games. He has scored in three successive games in the Premier League for the first time since joining the club.

Florian Wirtz will also play a key role in Liverpool’s attacking play. The German has been consistently creating chances for the team playing behind Isak in a 4-2-3-1 system. He has created nine chances against Brentford.

Arsenal will need to limit the space available to Liverpool’s forward players. Allowing Isak and Gakpo to combine freely could make an already difficult away fixture even harder.

Arsenal have had some positive news on their squad ahead of their Merseyside trip. Declan Rice and Riccardo Calafiori are both back in training.

Christos Tzolis has also trained after missing October. Noni Madueke has also not been ruled out by Arteta ahead of Sunday’s match after he missed the Fleetwood game.

They also have their own fitness issues to contend with. Iraola confirmed that Dominik Szoboszlai and Victor Munoz are both available after suffering injuries earlier in the month.

Jeramie Frimpong will be missing due to a hamstring issue but should be back after the international break. Hugo Ekitike, Geovanni Leoni and Conor Bradley are all still sidelined with longer-term injuries.

The presence of Rice will be key for Arsenal. He has been one of the best progressive midfielders in the Premier League, along with their captain, Martin Odegaard.

Despite losing just one of their last eight Premier League clashes against Liverpool, Arsenal are not as strong on the road.

The Gunners have not won any league games in their last 13 trips to Anfield. They have drawn five and lost eight. Their last win was a 2-0 victory back in the 2012/13 season.

They have also never gone 14 games unbeaten away from home in the league. That would be a great achievement.

However, there is good reason to believe they can do it. They have only conceded five clean sheets in the league this season. They have faced the least number of shots on target and the fewest expected goals.

Liverpool have also been on a strong run defensively with four clean sheets in their last six league games, so Arsenal must make their chances count.

A win would provide another key result against a direct rival, improving their position at the top of the Premier League table and finally putting an end to one of the clubs’ longest-running away day woes.

#2177. England vs Czechia: TV channel and kick-off time as Chelsea’s Morgan Rogers eyes Wembley start

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Chelsea supporters can watch England face Czechia on ITV1 and ITVX tonight, with Morgan Rogers hoping to return to the starting line-up at Wembley.

The attacker scored England’s final goal in their 7-0 victory over Croatia on Saturday after coming off the bench.

England’s Nations League fixture kicks off at 7.45pm BST on Tuesday 6 October at Wembley Stadium.

The FA’s official match guide confirms the date, venue and UK broadcast arrangements.

This is England’s final match of the extended September and October international window.

ITV1 will show the match live, with coverage beginning at 7pm BST, 45 minutes before kick-off.

Viewers can also stream the broadcast through ITVX’s live ITV service.

Rogers started England’s 2-0 victory in Czechia last week before Thomas Tuchel named him among the substitutes against Croatia.

He entered that match in the 69th minute and scored after collecting Jude Bellingham’s pass and rounding the goalkeeper.

Tonight’s starting XI remains unconfirmed. The FA expects team news around 75 minutes before kick-off, approximately 6.30pm BST.

After this fixture, attention returns to Chelsea’s next run of matches, beginning against Bournemouth at Stamford Bridge on Saturday.

#2176. Birmingham City, Celtic & Blackburn eye Chelsea man who could be 'better than Messi'

Blackburn Rovers and Birmingham City are both keen on a deal to sign Kendry Paez from Chelsea.

According to Teamtalk, the two Championship sides are both keen on the 19-year-old ahead of the January transfer window.

The teenager, who has been tipped to potentially be 'better than Messi' by ex-Ecuador international Carlos Tenorio, is currently a part of the first team squad at Stamford Bridge after returning early from a loan spell with River Plate.

However, he is not a part of Xabi Alonso’s plans at the moment, and has yet to play a single minute for the Spaniard this season.

The attacking-midfielder could be available in the new year, with the Premier League side seeking a loan move in order for Paez to gain valuable experience.

Chelsea signed the Ecuadorian in 2025 in a deal worth £17.3 million, but he has yet to make his debut for the club.

It’s believed that both Birmingham and Blackburn are keen on a deal to sign Paez when the winter market opens in January.

However, the pair will face competition from Scottish giants Celtic, who are also interested in signing the 19-year-old.

The playmaker previously spent time on loan with Strasbourg, with the French club owned by the same group as the Blues.

Jude and Jobe Bellingham have been named in Thomas Tuchel's senior Three Lions squad together for the first time

But he was only capable of making 15 appearances in Ligue 1, including four starts, scoring on just one occasion before going to River Plate January 2026.

It’s understood that Chelsea have been encouraged by his performances at underage level, and are now seeking a loan deal again in the upcoming January market.

Blackburn and Birmingham are both keen on a deal, but there are clubs from across Europe keeping tabs on the player’s progress, including Trabzonspor and KV Mechelen.

The Belgian side reportedly made an approach last summer, and are aiming to be at the front of the queue again in the new year.

Birmingham moved into top spot in the Championship table last weekend ahead of the November international break, with a 2-1 win against Swansea City lifting them ahead of Middlesbrough in second.

Meanwhile, Blackburn Rovers moved up to 13th with a 2-1 win of their own at home to West Ham, with eight points separating the two clubs chasing Paez’s signature.

Chris Davies’ side will be targeting promotion back to the Premier League in the second half of the campaign, while the Lancashire outfit will be aiming to break into the top eight.

Birmingham will return to action against Stoke City on 21 November, while Rovers will travel to face bitter rivals Burnley the following afternoon.

Paez wasn’t ready for top flight football in France last year, and his underwhelming performances reflected that.

But he’s now had time with Chelsea’s underage team to develop his game and better prepare for senior football, as he is still only 19 and moving from South America to Europe isn’t an easy transition.

Would Kendry Paez be a good signing for Blackburn or Birmingham? Comment your opinion below

There are no guarantees that his improvement is real because the step up from youth football to senior minutes is tough, but this move could be a gamble worth taking.

If Paez can make that step up, then he could bring the kind of attacking flair and creativity in the second half of the season that can bolster a promotion push for either Birmingham or Blackburn.

#2175. Michael Carrick handed lifeline ahead of Chelsea trip

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Michael Carrick has come under immense pressure as a disjointed Manchester United sit just two places above the relegation places.

Despite the scrutiny and the news of a possible sacking, Michael Carrick has been given another chance at Manchester United.

Michael Carrick will remain in charge of Manchester United for their Premier League trip to Chelsea on Saturday after the club decided to give their under-pressure head coach another opportunity to turn things around.

United have gone eight games without a win in all competitions, with Sunday’s humiliating 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth leaving them 16th in the Premier League table, just one point above the relegation zone.

Carrick’s position looked increasingly uncertain after the Bournemouth loss. As a result, United chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox were at Carrington on Monday to discuss the situation.

It has been reported by Sky Sports that United have considered other options internally as well to replace Micahel Carrick but for the weekend, they are sticking with Michael Carrick.

Carrick took training on Tuesday and the decision means he has at least one more match to convince the club’s hierarchy that he can get United back on track.

Gary Neville admitted he was surprised that Carrick had survived the immediate threat of losing his job, although he welcomed the decision.

“I am surprised, but I am happy because I was absolutely certain that what I witnessed on Sunday would be the end of Michael,” Neville said in Sky Sports.

The former United captain explained that Carrick’s actions after the Bournemouth defeat had made him think the manager was preparing to say goodbye to the supporters.

“It looked like he was walking round at the end on his own with the players behind him, like it was almost a goodbye lap,” Neville added.

Fans continued singing the manager’s name after the final whistle, while the rest of the team drew boos.

Despite his surprise, Gary Neville hopes Micahel Carrick can use the extra time to get a response from his squad.

“I hope he can somehow get those players to start playing for him again because they haven’t played for him in the last few weeks,” he said.

The trip to Chelsea now becomes a crucial test for Micahel Carrick but United need a result and a far better performance to ease the pressure at Old Trafford.
